# Scout Ledger

Persistent memory for the [Skill Scout](SKILL_SCOUT.md) job. One row per third-party marketing
skill/collection evaluated, so the scout compounds knowledge instead of re-scanning the same
sources. **Verdicts:** `adopted-new` (added a new skill), `enhanced-ours` (improved one of ours
using ideas from it), `dismissed` (with reason), `watch` (revisit / proposed for human review).

Do not delete rows — they are the audit trail of what we looked at and why we did or didn't act.

## 2026-07-26 — focus: paid media / paid social (5 sources → 1 enhance, 1 watch, 2 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: content/SEO-AEO shipped 2026-07-26, email/analytics/ops covered 2026-07-25, so paid and PMM/sales were tied at 2026-07-21. Paid won on queue depth — it held two open backlog enhancements to PMM/sales' one.

| 2026-07-26 | [AgriciDaniel/claude-ads](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-ads) (~7.5k★) | MIT | Paid / PPC | **enhanced-ours** | Re-checked after 2026-07-21 (it has grown substantially: 12 platforms, capability-gated mutations, versioned JSON reports). Two ideas were genuinely worth learning and we had neither: (a) a **four-state control model** — `pass`/`fail`/`unknown`/`not_applicable`, where unknown never rounds to pass; (b) **evidence coverage graded separately from health**, with ≥80% graded / 60–79% provisional / <60% insufficient, so an audit can't present a confident score over data it couldn't see. Also the capability-gate shape for writes (explicit IDs, before/after diff, approval ceilings, idempotency, rollback, pre-mutation state check). Ideas only; wrote the section from scratch. |
| 2026-07-26 | [fourteenwm/ppc-ai-skills](https://github.com/fourteenwm/ppc-ai-skills) (55★) | MIT | Paid / PPC | **enhanced-ours** | Its search-term work is the sharpest thing in the paid corner of this market: SQR classifier → SQR pipeline with a **multi-run consensus** classification and a human review gate → a **negative-conflict finder** that audits across ad group / campaign / shared list / account for negatives blocking positives. Also 42-point GREEN/YELLOW/RED account diagnostic with an estimated monthly waste figure. Adopted as the "search-term loop" (cadence → n-gram rollup → intent buckets → double-classify → conflict check → level choice → gated apply → measure both directions). Ideas only. |
| 2026-07-26 | [hyperfx-ai/marketing-skills](https://github.com/hyperfx-ai/marketing-skills) (66★, 24 skills incl. 8 paid-ads platforms) | MIT | Paid / social ads | **watch** | Nothing textually adoptable — it's MCP-wired executable skills over 100+ integrations, a different architecture from our advisory personas. Its one transferable idea (every action can require approval before it runs; campaigns created paused) reinforced the gate we shipped and is credited there. Second independent market signal, after OpenClaudia on 2026-07-25, that the field is moving advisory → executable; strengthens the case for promoting the P2 "MCP tool recipes" item to P1. |
| 2026-07-26 | [Hainrixz/claude-ads](https://github.com/Hainrixz/claude-ads) | MIT | Paid / PPC | **dismissed** (duplicate) | Same lineage and near-identical scope to `AgriciDaniel/claude-ads` with a self-updating `/ads update` refresh command layered on. ## 2026-07-28 — focus: PMM / sales / GTM (6 sources → 1 enhance, 4 dismissed, 1 watch-refresh)

Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM/sales/GTM was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-21, while content/SEO-AEO (07-26), email/analytics/ops (07-25 → shipped 07-27), and paid/social (07-26) have each had a dedicated run since.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-07-28 | [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills) (205 skills / 24 categories, pushed 2026-07-20) | MIT | Sales / GTM | **enhanced-ours** | The find of the run is its `sales-revops/buyer-indecision` skill, which surfaces **the JOLT Effect** (Dixon & McKenna, 2022) — a framework we had *no* coverage of. Verified the gap: our `sales-deal-strategist` models loss as competitor-won or status-quo-preferred, and "status quo" appears exactly 3 times repo-wide, always as a value problem. The research says the majority of no-decision losses are the opposite: buyers who *want* to buy and can't commit, for whom the cost-of-inaction reflex actively backfires. Shipped as "The Third Loss Mode" in both dual-located copies — the four JOLT moves adapted to multi-threaded enterprise deals, a champion-runs-the-play adaptation that is ours, a loss-classification discipline, and a new `No-Decision Loss Mix` success metric. Ideas only; framework attributed to its authors, stats cited to two Dixon-affiliated sources with the 84% figure flagged as inconsistently framed between them. Also noted for future runs: `abm/multi-thread-orchestration`, `sales-revops/transparency-selling`, `sales-plays/*-signal-play`. |

That is architecturally the same bet we already shipped as `templates/brand-context.md` (Step 0 in all 13 skills) plus the `loops/` state ledgers. Skill-level content is thinner than our per-discipline agents. Nothing to adopt. |

## 2026-07-30 — focus: email / analytics / marketing-ops (7 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 4 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: email/analytics/ops was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-25 (shipped 07-27), while paid/social and content/SEO-AEO both ran 07-26 and PMM/sales/GTM ran 07-28.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-07-30 | [NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills](https://github.com/NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills) (37★, 34 skills, pushed 2026-07-23) | MIT | Marketing ops / RevOps | **enhanced-ours** | The find of the run is its `marketing-operations` skill, which models lead scoring as **two independent axes with an AND-gate** (`MQL = Fit ≥ F AND Engagement ≥ E`) rather than one additive score. Verified the gap: our repo prescribed a **single 0–100 score with a 50+ handoff threshold** (`email-automation-engineer` deliverable) and **"MQL at 20+ points"** (`phase-2-foundation`), listing engagement/demographic/firmographic/behavioral as inputs *summed into one number*. That shape lets engagement compensate for fit — the actual mechanism behind "sales says our leads are garbage." Shipped as **"Scoring Fit and Engagement as Two Axes, Not One Number"** in both dual-located copies of `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` (the owner of scoring-model *design*, vs. the automation engineer who owns implementation): the AND-gate, a four-quadrant disposition table where low-fit/high-engagement is investigated-not-routed, a decay-engagement-never-fit rule, recycled-vs-disqualified reason codes, and challenger-versioning for a live model. Ideas only. Its own MQL-acceptance figures ("62% to 81%", presented as *practice-based*) were **deliberately not reproduced**; platform behavior instead cited to Adobe Experience League and HubSpot's Knowledge Base (read 2026-07-30). Noted for future runs: `revops-data-governance` (field-prefix naming by owning team, data dictionary, deprecation policy) and `gtm-data-architecture`. |
| 2026-07-30 | [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (2.5k★, 120 skills, pushed 2026-07-29) | Apache-2.0 | Email (16 skills) | **watch** | The largest permissively-licensed email corpus surveyed to date — a four-stage SEND loop (setup / nurture / engage / deliver) plus a `protocol/` layer of shared registries. Two ideas are genuinely worth a future run and we have neither: (a) `preference-frequency-manager` — a **frequency opt-down ladder** (weekly → monthly → pause → sunset) as a structured alternative to a hard unsubscribe, mapped to suppression rules; and (b) `protocol/consent-registry` — consent/suppression as a **durable shared record** other skills read, rather than state living inside one ESP. We have preference centers mentioned in `email-lifecycle-architect` but no opt-down ladder, and "sunset" appears repo-wide only in a social agent. Not actioned this run (one change per run); filed to backlog. Note: skill bodies are bilingual EN/ZH with heavy repo-specific scaffolding (SEND N-dimension vetoes, EQS scores) that does not transfer — ideas only, and Apache-2.0 notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation. |
| 2026-07-30 | [metrikia-io/marketing-skills](https://github.com/metrikia-io/marketing-skills) (1★, pushed 2026-07-28) | MIT | Analytics / attribution | **watch** | Single-purpose `attribution-model-analysis` skill with `references/model-formulas.md` and `references/data-preparation.md`. Small and new, but the *shape* — attribution math split into a formula reference separate from the persona — is worth re-checking as it matures. Our `paid-media-attribution-analyst` already carries the Bayesian-MMM backbone adopted 2026-07-21, so nothing to adopt now. |

## 2026-07-30 (second run) — focus: content / SEO-AEO-GEO (10 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 6 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: content/SEO-AEO-GEO and paid/social were tied at 2026-07-26; content/SEO won the tiebreak on queue depth (paid shipped *two* enhancements that day and has an empty queue; the SEO corner still holds the open `seo-programmatic-strategist` proposal) and because the AEO/GEO half of the market moves fastest.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-07-30 | [zubair-trabzada/geo-seo-claude](https://github.com/zubair-trabzada/geo-seo-claude) (9.2k★, 15 skills, pushed 2026-05-27) | MIT | AEO / GEO | **enhanced-ours** | The find of the run is that it ships a dedicated `geo-crawlers` skill — **AI-crawler access as a first-class AEO dimension**, tiered by bot purpose. Verified the gap: we had *zero* coverage. `GPTBot` appeared **nowhere** in the repo, "off-site" nowhere, and robots.txt appeared only in generic technical-SEO crawl-budget lines with no AI-agent awareness at all. That made the whole `seo-ai-search-optimizer` stack — Field Guide and the citability rubric shipped 07-26 — rest on an unaudited assumption that the engine can fetch the page. Shipped as **"Access Before Citation: Auditing AI Crawler Reachability"** in both dual-located copies, plus a new Critical Rule 9. Its own tiering was **not** adopted: its classification puts `GPTBot` on the search/retrieval side, which contradicts OpenAI's own docs (GPTBot = training; `OAI-SearchBot` = ChatGPT search), so our table was rebuilt from each operator's primary documentation. Two layers it does not cover were added by us: **edge/WAF enforcement** (robots.txt says allow, the CDN returns 403) and **empirical log verification** (reachable / blocked / never-seen, with never-seen never rounding to reachable, and spoof-checking user agents against published IP ranges). Ideas only. |

## 2026-07-30 (third run) — focus: paid media / social (6 sources → 1 enhance, 1 watch, 4 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: paid/social was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-26, while email/analytics/ops and content/SEO-AEO-GEO both ran 2026-07-30 and PMM/sales/GTM ran 2026-07-28. Paid entered this run with an empty enhancement queue (both 07-26 items shipped that day).

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-07-30 | [AgriciDaniel/claude-ads](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-ads) — `skills/ads-test` | MIT | Paid / experimentation | **enhanced-ours** | Third mine of this repo (07-21 noted, 07-26 gave the four-state control model to `paid-media-ppc-strategist`), this time its **experiment** module, which we had never opened. Its discipline is *pre-registration*: declare the minimum detectable effect and the stopping rule before launch, disclose the assumptions inside the sample-size calculation rather than hiding them in a calculator, hold to one decision surface per experiment, and explicitly refuse to peek-and-stop on a favourable result or to call underpowered noise a winner. Verified the gap by grep first: **`underpowered` and `minimum detectable` appeared zero times repo-wide**, while the repo prescribes 95%-significance testing in nine places (`marketing-analytics/SKILL.md` even fixes "100 conversions per variant" as the bar). Nothing anywhere handled the case a B2B account actually lives in — *the test cannot be powered*. Worse, `paid-media-creative-strategist`'s own success metrics were internally contradictory: "40%+ lift with 95% statistical confidence within 60 days" alongside "3-5 independent creative tests monthly," which at B2B volumes are mutually exclusive and invite reading noise as signal. Shipped as **"Power Before Verdict: Testing at B2B Volumes"** in both dual-located copies + a new Critical Rule 9, and rewrote the two contradictory success metrics (plus added a `Test validity rate`). Ideas only. |
| 2026-07-30 | [borghei/Claude-Skills](https://github.com/borghei/Claude-Skills) (433★, 368 skills, pushed 2026-07-21) | **MIT + Commons Clause** (restrictive — ideas-only) | Paid / ad creative | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | Its `marketing/ad-creative` skill contributes the one idea the section needed to be actionable rather than merely cautionary: a **variable-impact hierarchy** (concept > hook > visual > body > CTA) that says which test deserves a slot. Re-ranked by us for B2B, where *offer* and *audience* outrank everything creative, and reframed around MDE — the bottom of the list is not merely lower-leverage, it is arithmetically untestable at B2B volumes. Its own testing guidance stops at a 1,000-impression floor and a 95%-confidence bar with no underpowered handling, so nothing there was adopted. **Commons Clause makes this non-free** — ideas-only under our rules, no text reused, license flagged in the attribution. |

That is the reference-class question our `ops-quality-assurance` Four U's rubric (shipped 07-26) does not ask. Not actioned — the repo is creator-economy shaped (Reels scripts, YouTube thumbnails, clickbait hook generators, meme pinned-comments) and hostile to B2B trust-building, so only the scoring-reference-class idea transfers, and one change per run. ## 2026-07-31 — focus: PMM / sales / GTM (7 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 4 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM/sales/GTM was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-28, while email/analytics/ops, content/SEO-AEO-GEO, and paid/social all ran on 2026-07-30. The PMM/sales queue also entered this run empty — the LeadMagic mine was fully worked out on 07-28 (JOLT ✓, multi-thread ✓, transparency ✓).

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-07-31 | [pmalliance/product-marketing-skills](https://github.com/pmalliance/product-marketing-skills) (58★, pushed 2026-07-10) | MIT | PMM | **enhanced-ours** | Low star count but high credibility — it is the Product Marketing Alliance's own skill set. Its `pmm-signal-pack` is built on one organizing idea: PMM work that only becomes true *in bulk*, where the finding exists in the aggregate and a sample of a dozen actively misleads. The find of the run is `customer-language-bank` — read voice-of-customer material in volume, rank the vocabulary buyers actually use **by frequency**, then **diff it against your own live copy** to find where your words and theirs have parted. Gap grep-verified before writing: `"buyer's own words"`, `"review mining"`, `"language bank"`, and `"word cloud"` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; `verbatim` hit only a client-satisfaction-survey deliverable and one workflow example; every `"their own words"` / `"customer's own words"` hit was about *internal teams articulating positioning*, not about mining buyer vocabulary. `pmm-messaging-architect`'s Rule 1 sends you to customer research for what buyers *believe matters* and Rule 4 governs *proof* — nothing anywhere owned where the **words** come from, which is a real hole in that agent's own stated logic ("great messaging doesn't come from internal product knowledge"). Shipped as **"Sourcing the Vocabulary: Building a Customer Language Bank"** + Critical Rule 9, a new deliverable, and a `Vocabulary Grounding` success metric, in both dual-located copies. Ideas only, written from scratch. **Three layers the source does not have were added by us:** (a) a **corpus-bias weighting table** — every source is skewed in a known direction and pooling them undifferentiated produces a vocabulary that reads authoritative and isn't, with sales calls flagged as the *circular* corpus (the buyer echoes the rep's framing back, so hearing your own term there is not validation); (b) the **buyer-vs-user partition**, the B2B-specific point that the reviewer is usually the user while the signer is the economic buyer, so review-mined language aimed at a homepage is user vocabulary pointed at a buyer; (c) an **adopt / bridge / refuse** decision rule making explicit that frequency is evidence, not a mandate — a competitor's category name or a commodity label can be the market's most frequent term and still lose you the argument, and those route to positioning, not copy. G2's $100 incentive cap and its labeling of confirmed incentivized reviews cited to [G2's Community Guidelines](https://legal.g2.com/community-guidelines), read 2026-07-31; corpus-skew characterizations are labeled in-file as methodological judgments, not measured figures. |
| 2026-07-31 | [pmalliance/product-marketing-skills](https://github.com/pmalliance/product-marketing-skills) — `message-consistency-check` | MIT | PMM | **watch** | The same repo's second-best idea: read *every* customer-facing surface together (site, decks, one-pagers, nurture, ads, product copy) and report where the category claimed, the value led with, the audience addressed, and the proof cited diverge — ranked by surface visibility and stakes. Distinct from the vocabulary diff shipped today: that one compares our copy to *buyers' words*, this compares our copy to *itself*. Partial coverage exists — `pmm-messaging-architect` Rule 6 mandates a quarterly coherence audit and a `Message Consistency Score` metric — but the rule names the surfaces and stops; there is no method for *how* to compare, no prioritization by stakes, and nothing on the post-repositioning case where old copy survives in low-traffic corners. Not actioned (one change per run); filed to backlog. |
| 2026-07-31 | [pmalliance/product-marketing-skills](https://github.com/pmalliance/product-marketing-skills) — `claim-check`, `win-loss-reasons`, `objection-library` | MIT | PMM / sales | **dismissed** (have) | Checked all three against our inventory and each is already owned at comparable or greater depth. `claim-check` (every claim graded supported / thin / unsupported against the evidence base) → `pmm-messaging-architect` Rule 4 + Rule 8 + the Proof Point Library deliverable + the "Zero unsubstantiated claims" metric; its supported/thin/unsupported taxonomy is also the four-state control pattern we already adopted repo-wide on 07-26. `win-loss-reasons` (separate what buyers *said* from what the pattern *shows*) → `pmm-competitive-intelligence` Rule 5 already makes exactly that move at the source level ("Base Win/Loss Analysis on Customer Interviews, **Not Sales Claims**") plus a 20+ page quarterly report, and the JOLT no-decision classification shipped 07-28 covers the loss-mode half. `objection-library` → `sales-enablement-content-creator` and `sales-discovery-coach`. No duplication. |

Its GTM-adjacent plugins (`pm-go-to-market`: beachhead-segment, gtm-motions, growth-loops, ICP, competitive-battlecard; `pm-market-research`: market-sizing, segments, personas, journey maps; `pm-marketing-growth`: north-star-metric, positioning-ideas) are written for a PM deciding *what to build*, and read thin against our per-discipline marketing agents where they overlap at all. Star count is not substance for our purpose. Distribution target, not a source. |

## 2026-08-01 — focus: email / analytics / marketing-ops (8 sources → 1 enhance, 1 watch, 6 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: email/analytics/ops was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-30, the earliest of that day's three runs (content/SEO and paid/social followed it), and PMM/sales/GTM ran 2026-07-31. Its queue also entered this run essentially empty — the `aaron-he-zhu` opt-down ladder shipped 07-30 and the `revops-data-governance` field-layer item shipped 07-30, leaving only the architectural consent-registry scope question.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-08-01 | [cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills](https://github.com/cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills) (82★, pushed 2026-06-16) — `/gtm-audit` | MIT per README (GitHub API reports no license file); the skill itself is **paywalled** ($9/mo Cogny MCP) | Analytics / measurement | **enhanced-ours** | Second mine of this repo (07-25 → shipped the GA4 config audit 07-27 from its paywalled `/ga4-audit`; same pattern here). Its `/gtm-audit` container check names **Consent Mode** alongside duplicate/orphaned tags, trigger coverage, and naming hygiene. The skill is not evaluable or adoptable — it needs a paid account and their MCP servers — but the *dimension* was a hard gap. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`Consent Mode` returned zero hits repo-wide**, as did `modeled conversion` and `tag governance`; `Tag Manager` appeared only as a setup checklist line in two strategy playbooks; the sole mention of consent in the whole analytics stack was a half-clause naming "consent-mode gaps" as one possible cause of GA4 *Unassigned* traffic. That meant the entire GA4 instrumentation audit shipped 07-27 rested on an unaudited assumption that the data **arrived** — the one measurement failure that produces no error, no `(not set)` row, and no warning, just a smaller correct-looking number. Shipped as **"Consent Before Collection: Separating Observed Data from Modeled and Missing"** + Critical Rule 9 + a `Consent Coverage Transparency` success metric in both dual-located copies of `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`. Ideas/dimension only; written from scratch; every fact cited to Google primary docs (Tag Platform, Analytics Help, Google Ads Help) read 2026-08-01. **The find of the run is ours, not the source's:** GA4 behavioral modeling requires ≥1,000 events/day denied for 7 consecutive days *and* ≥1,000 daily users granted for 7 of the previous 28 — thresholds a B2B SaaS site with a named-account motion is routinely one to two orders of magnitude below, so the standing default for our ICP is "advanced consent mode implemented, cookieless pings sent, nothing modeled back" — paying advanced's cost for basic's coverage. Labeled in-file as our reasoning over Google's published requirements, not a figure Google publishes. Three failure modes (banner-change confound, ratios degrading worse than counts, global average hiding the regional hole) and the observed/modeled/missing decomposition are also ours. |

It has grown into the most jurisdictionally-thorough marketing collection surveyed to date — compliance rules across 16 markets, C2PA provenance signing, EU AI Act Art. 50 readiness against the June 2026 final guidelines. Its analytics skills (`incrementality-testing`, `cohort-analysis`, `churn-risk`, `attribution-report`) were checked against our inventory and are **covered**: incrementality/holdout sits in `paid-media-attribution-analyst` (Bayesian MMM, 07-21), cohort analysis in `analytics-performance-analyst` + the email agents, churn across seven agents. Nothing adoptable this run. Two things make it worth a real re-read on the next ops run: its *Two-Views Model* (unbiased market view held alongside a client-validated view) and its *Living Project Instruction File* propagating corrections downstream, both of which are architecture questions adjacent to our `brand-context.md`. Discipline chosen by rotation: content/SEO-AEO-GEO was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-30 (the second of that day's three runs, ahead of paid/social), while PMM/sales/GTM ran 07-31 and email/analytics/ops ran 08-01.

| 2026-08-02 | [AgriciDaniel/claude-blog](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-blog) (1,543★, 30 sub-skills / 5 agents, pushed 2026-07-23) | MIT | Content / blog SEO | **enhanced-ours** | Fourth repo from this author but the **first time this one has been opened** (claude-ads mined 07-21/07-26/07-30, claude-seo 07-21). The find is its `blog-decay` skill: content decay as a **period-over-period comparison with a branch that is allowed not to end in an edit** — refresh / investigate query shift / consolidate-redirect / prune, with pre-action validation of indexation, canonicals, backlinks, and seasonality *before* any action. Gap grep-verified first: **`content decay`, `dropped out`, `deindex`, `content inventory`, and `historical optimization` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `redirect` and `canonical` existed only in `seo-technical-auditor` (technical, not content-lifecycle) and `retire` only in the analytics agents (field and campaign retirement). That left `seo-content-optimizer` — an agent whose entire identity is "80% of SEO wins come from improving existing content" — with a **calendar-driven** trigger (Rule 7: quarterly review of the top 100) and **no branch that does not end in an edit**: consolidation was one clause inside one deliverable, and pruning did not exist. Shipped as **"Reading a Decline Before Prescribing a Fix"** + Critical Rule 9 + a `Content Decay Triage` deliverable + two success metrics, in both dual-located copies. Its own 20/40/60% severity tiers were **not adopted** — a percentage threshold with no absolute floor is actively wrong at B2B volumes. Ideas only, written from scratch. |

## 2026-08-03 — focus: paid media / social (9 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 6 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: paid/social was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-30 (the third of that day's three runs), the oldest of the four — PMM/sales/GTM ran 07-31, email/analytics/ops 08-01, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-02. Paid also entered this run with an empty enhancement queue (the power-discipline item shipped 07-30).

| 2026-08-03 | [mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills](https://github.com/mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills) (1★, 12 skills, pushed 2026-07-23) | MIT | Paid social / LinkedIn | **enhanced-ours** | Lowest star count of anything ever adopted here, and the substance is real — star count is not evidence, and this is the first source surveyed that is *only* LinkedIn Ads, the channel that matters most for B2B SaaS paid. The find is its `demographics-leak-finder` + `wasted-spend-finder` pair, whose organizing idea is that **targeting is a request and delivery is what happened** — audit the demographics breakdown against a written ICP inclusion set to find where budget actually went, and treat Audience Expansion and the LinkedIn Audience Network as the first two suspects. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`demographics report` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `seniority` returned 4, *all* in customer-research recruiting and ghostwriting cadence and **none in any paid-media agent**; `audience expansion` returned 6, every one meaning *lookalike expansion as a growth tactic*, never LinkedIn's setting; `Audience Network` returned 2, both the same dual-located `phase-4-launch` line that recommends *including* Meta's Audience Network for "broader reach" with no B2B caveat. So `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` — an agent whose stated superpower is "targeting precision that reaches ideal customer profiles while eliminating waste" — had Rules 4 and 8 governing who you *select* and **no method whatever for checking that delivery honored it**, against two settings LinkedIn enables by default. Shipped as **"Delivery Is Not Targeting: Auditing Who Actually Received the Budget"** + Critical Rule 9 + a `Delivery-vs-Targeting Audit` deliverable + an `On-ICP delivery share` metric, in both dual-located copies. Ideas only, written from scratch. **Four layers the source does not have are ours:** (a) **the two settings fail in opposite directions and need different evidence** — Expansion corrupts *who* (visible in demographics), LAN corrupts *where* (invisible there, visible only in the placement split), and reading one report to conclude about both is the actual analytical error; (b) **the ABM enumeration argument** — a matched account list is a finite enumeration, not a description, so "members similar to your audience" cannot be in it; Expansion on an ABM campaign doesn't widen the list, it abandons it while still reporting under its name; (c) **the suppression consequence** — LinkedIn hides demographic facets with too little data "to protect member identity," and because that floor hides *small* slices, a long tail of many small off-ICP slices is precisely the leak pattern the report is least able to show, so fragmented leakage reads as a clean report (our standing *unknown never rounds to observed* discipline, applied again); (d) **the over-narrow inverse tell** — near-zero off-ICP delivery usually means an unservable audience, not perfect targeting. Also noted: LinkedIn's own docs scope Expansion to "an upper-funnel marketing strategy," so turning it off on ABM/BOFU *applies* the vendor's guidance rather than defying it. All platform behavior quoted from and cited to four LinkedIn Marketing Solutions Help articles (a418929, a420372, a424171, a427359), read 2026-08-03; **no waste percentage asserted** — the 20–30% / 30–40% figures circulating for LAN and Expansion trace to agency blogs, not to LinkedIn, and were deliberately not reproduced. |
| 2026-08-03 | [mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills](https://github.com/mardab96/linkedin-ads-claude-skills) — `cpl-vs-crm-reconciliation`, `conversion-tracking-audit` | MIT | Paid / measurement | **watch** | The same repo's second-best idea and a partial gap. `paid-media-attribution-analyst` already owns platform→CRM reconciliation at the *object* level (Rule 2 + a 95%-of-conversions-map metric + a CRM Integration & Data Mapping deliverable), so the discipline is not absent — but the LinkedIn-specific sharp edge is that **platform-reported CPL and CRM-derived CPL for the same campaign routinely disagree**, and the skill's move is to reconcile them per campaign and report the *gap itself* as the finding rather than picking whichever number is more flattering. Also its ordering rule, which is the transferable part: run the tracking audit *first*, because "a waste verdict on top of broken measurement is a confident wrong answer." Not actioned — one change per run, and the seam between the Attribution Analyst and the Social Ads Specialist needs deciding first. Filed to backlog. |
| 2026-08-03 | [olson-adam/ad-mastermind](https://github.com/olson-adam/ad-mastermind) (0★, pushed 2026-07-30) | MIT | Paid / B2B creative | **watch** | Zero stars and brand-new, but the only genuinely novel *method* surfaced this run: grade ad concepts by **blind judging with a discrimination control** — a deliberately weak decoy concept seeded into the judged set, where a judge that fails to rank the decoy last invalidates the whole scoring round as flattery rather than evaluation. That is a direct answer to the failure mode our `ops-quality-assurance` reference-class gate (shipped 07-31) names but cannot detect: a generous scorer. Also does live competitor-ad field scans against the Meta and LinkedIn ad libraries, which is public-source competitive creative intel we have no owner for. Too new and too unproven to adopt on sight; re-check on the next paid run and, if it survives, the decoy-control idea belongs on `ops-quality-assurance` rather than on a paid agent. Filed to backlog. |

## 2026-08-04 — focus: PMM / sales / GTM (11 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 8 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM/sales/GTM was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-07-31, the oldest of the four — email/analytics/ops ran 08-01, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-02, paid/social 08-03. The PMM/sales queue also entered this run empty: the `pmalliance` mine was fully worked on 07-31 (language bank shipped, consistency method shipped, three skills verified already-covered).

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-08-04 | [matteotitta/genesys-skills](https://github.com/matteotitta/genesys-skills) (26★, 168 skills, pushed 2026-07-31) | MIT | PMM / GTM | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting, and the closest scope match to this repo ever surveyed — "168 production Claude Code skills for B2B SaaS go-to-market," organized as `meta/` + `primitives/` across content, outbound, paid, SEO/AEO, PMM, sales enablement. Low star count, real substance; star count has never been our evidence. The find is its `product-marketing/strategy/vertical-messaging` skill, whose organizing idea is that segment messaging is a **routing problem, not an authoring problem**: lock the messaging library, and per segment vary only *which pillar leads* and *the one-to-two-line entry point*, routed by a **buying trigger** ("what changed in this buyer's world") rather than standing pain. Carries two rules worth learning — an "iron law" that no trigger ships without voice-of-customer evidence or an estimated flag, and a **swap test** (an entry point that still reads fine when moved to another segment is a generic tagline occupying a segment row). Gap grep-verified before writing: `vertical messaging`, `industry messaging`, and `swap test` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; every `vertical` hit was an ICP *targeting* dimension (`sales-outbound-strategist` line 44 "industry verticals with highest fit"; `phase-1-strategy` line 316 "Industries (vertical focus)") or a reference-account *tag* (`pmm-customer-advocacy` line 44), and the sole messaging mention was one clause inside a deliverable ("industry/vertical pages"). So the hole sat inside `pmm-messaging-architect`'s own Rule 3, which names **two** variation axes — persona and buying moment — and omits the third that sales asks for constantly. Shipped as **"Messaging by Segment: Route the Entry Point, Don't Fork the House"** + Critical Rule 10 + a `Segment Entry-Point Routing Map` deliverable + a `Segment Routing Integrity` metric, in both dual-located copies. Ideas only, written from scratch. **Six layers the source does not have are ours:** (a) the **two failure modes and their maintenance cost** — one averaged message that describes no one, versus a per-vertical fork that makes every repositioning an N-times job and hands the surface-against-surface audit shipped 07-31 N× the pairs to check; (b) an **axis-recognizability test** — pick one segment axis and pick it on whether a buyer would use it to describe themselves unprompted, or you have routed messaging on a CRM reporting dimension; (c) **trigger perishability** — a message house is reviewed semi-annually because positioning moves slowly, but a trigger map is built on *dated events* and a passed compliance deadline reads exactly as persuasive as it did when true, so every trigger carries a date and a review class (deadline / wave / structural) and the map is re-verified quarterly; (d) the **base-rate counterfactual** — the segment's non-buyers lived through the same regulation and the same audit season, so a trigger earns its row only if it appears disproportionately in accounts that actually entered a cycle, read against win/loss with `pmm-competitive-intelligence`; (e) the **segment-proof credibility check** — naming a vertical promises vertical evidence, and `pmm-customer-advocacy` already tags reference accounts by vertical/industry, so an unbacked row either softens to a segment-*framed* claim on cross-industry proof or waits, with the missing reference logged as a gap; (f) the **merge test**, the inverse of the swap test — two segments routing to the same pillar with interchangeable entry points are one segment. The source's **numeric confidence thresholds (≥60% verified rows / ≤10% estimated) were deliberately not adopted** — an arbitrary percentage bar that a three-segment map cannot meaningfully meet; replaced with a per-row, per-surface rule under the repo's standing *inferred never rounds to verified* discipline. No figures asserted. |
| 2026-08-04 | [Fearofsnakes/pmm-skillset](https://github.com/Fearofsnakes/pmm-skillset) (25★, 9 skills, pushed 2026-04-15) | MIT | PMM | **watch** | A tight, PMM-only tiered set (tier-1 ICP/positioning/messaging-hierarchy → tier-2 message-market-fit/VOC-synthesis/competitive-landing-page → tier-3 battlecard/launch-brief/sales-narrative/feature-announcement). Its `message-market-fit` skill is the one worth returning to and it is a **real partial gap**: our Rule 5 says "test messaging with sales teams and customers before rollout … never launch messaging you haven't validated" and then stops — it names the audiences and gives no method. The source supplies three things we lack: a **five-pillar audit score** (ICP alignment / differentiation / clarity / emotion / consistency) applied per asset; a **pre-test feasibility gate** on whether the test can even run (is the ICP targetable, can the channel produce enough qualified interactions per variant in the window, is measurement past clicks); and a **composite signal scorecard** that declares a winner on a majority of eight mixed quantitative *and* qualitative signals (reply rate, landing conversion, scroll depth, 5-second clarity test, objection reduction, customer preference, "echo moments" where a prospect repeats your phrasing back). That last one is the B2B-shaped answer to the problem `paid-media-creative-strategist` already owns from the other side — the power-discipline shipped 2026-07-30 says an underpowered test cannot declare a winner, and a B2B messaging test is almost always underpowered, so qualitative signals are not a consolation prize but the actual instrument. Not actioned — one change per run, and the seam with the Creative Strategist's pre-registration discipline should be decided before writing. Filed to backlog. Repo is ~4 months stale but inside the ~6-month bar. |

## 2026-08-05 — focus: email / analytics / marketing-ops (14 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 11 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: email/analytics/ops was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-01, the oldest of the four — content/SEO-AEO-GEO ran 08-02, paid/social 08-03, PMM/sales/GTM 08-04. Its queue also entered this run empty: every curation item in this discipline is closed (opt-down ladder 07-30, field-layer governance 07-30, consent audit 08-01, container-hygiene scope decision 08-02, consent-registry scope decision 08-04).

| 2026-08-05 | [krillinai/growth-skills](https://github.com/krillinai/growth-skills) (30★, 86 skills, pushed 2026-07-28) — `growth-anomaly-investigation` | MIT (verified via GitHub license API) | Analytics / measurement | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting; built by clawee.ai and only three weeks old, but the analytics corner is the most rigorous surveyed in this discipline. The find is its `growth-anomaly-investigation` skill, whose organizing idea is that an anomaly resolves into one of **five declared verdicts** — `measurement issue`, `behavioral change`, `mixed`, `expected variation`, `unresolved` — with the *measurement* branch ranked ahead of any behavioral cause and `unresolved` permitted as a **terminal** state. Also carries a comparability freeze before any comparison, parent-total reconciliation on every decomposition, and an explicit anti-fishing rule ("do not slice until a tiny group looks extreme"). Gap grep-verified before writing: **`measurement issue`, `expected variation`, `series break`, `like-for-like`, `multiple comparison`, `immature`, `outcome window`, and `restate` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `root cause` hit `analytics-performance-analyst` only at Rule 7 itself and `confounding` only at Rule 5 (experiments). So Rule 7 — "investigate and document the cause. Was there a campaign change, external event, technical issue, or market shift?" — offered four candidates, three of which answer *why the world moved*, and never asked whether it moved; and it never defined *significantly*. Shipped as **"Reading a Change Before Explaining It"** + Critical Rule 9 + an `Anomaly Investigation Record` deliverable in both dual-located copies of `analytics-performance-analyst` — **the first enhancement this agent has ever received** (69 lines, untouched since the first pass). Ideas only, written from scratch. **Four layers the source does not have are ours:** (a) the **series-break register and its routing table** — in a B2B SaaS stack the instrument changes are *enumerable*, and this repo is uniquely able to enumerate them because its own shipped sections govern them (consent posture 08-01, GA4 key-event/channel-grouping config 07-27, the two-axis scoring model 07-30, the field-deprecation lifecycle 07-30, attribution windows 07-21, LinkedIn Expansion/LAN delivery 08-03), so each row names the metric that moves with **zero** behavioral change and the owning agent to confirm with; (b) the **B2B maturity argument** — outcome windows run in weeks-to-months against a fixed reporting calendar, so the freshest column of every cohort-outcome report is understated *by construction, every period, forever*, producing a permanent phantom downtrend and a phantom recovery on re-read, which is the single most-misreported "decline" in this ICP; (c) the **absolute-floor rule** — B2B numbers are small, eight demos to five is a 38% collapse and an ordinary month, so a percentage threshold with no minimum count is a small-denominator amplifier (the same call the content-decay triage made on 08-02 when it refused the source's 20/40/60% tiers); (d) the **misdiagnosis asymmetry** — calling a tracking break a market shift re-plans budget against nothing, calling a market shift a tracking break drains the funnel while everyone waits for a fix, so the report states which direction the evidence rules out. **Two internal contradictions fixed in passing** (same call as `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30): the "95%+ of anomalies within 48 hours" metric, structurally impossible for long-window metrics and an invitation to fabricate a cause on deadline, is now set per metric against its own maturity; and "segment analysis reveals 15-25% performance variation," which made finding variation a **quota** against Rule 3's mandatory 5-7 segments, is now a finding to qualify with post-hoc splits labeled exploratory. **No figures asserted** — expected ranges are stated in-file as properties of an account's own history that must be measured, not imported. |

Partial coverage exists and is scattered: `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` owns the GA4 config audit (07-27), consent (08-01), and the field layer (07-30), and `analytics-performance-analyst` Rule 1 requires documented metric definitions — but nothing owns the instrumentation *contract* that precedes all of them, and "tracking plan" appears repo-wide only as a one-line deliverable bullet in two playbooks and a handoff checklist, with `event taxonomy` and `event schema` at zero. Not actioned — one change per run, and the section shipped today explicitly *depends* on the series-break register that a tracking-plan owner would properly maintain, so the seam (Ops Architect section vs. something larger) should be decided before writing. ## 2026-08-06 — focus: content / SEO-AEO-GEO (17 sources → 1 enhance (+1 co-credit), 2 watch, 13 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: content/SEO-AEO-GEO was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-02, the oldest of the four — paid/social ran 08-03, PMM/sales/GTM 08-04, email/analytics/ops 08-05. Its queue also entered this run empty: the decay triage (08-02), the cannibalization audit (08-02), the crawler-access section (07-30), and the AEO↔Reddit seam (08-02) are all closed, leaving only two un-actioned `watch` notes (`cognitive-load`, the `blog-flow` prompt library).

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-08-06 | [jstanx/aeo-toolkit](https://github.com/jstanx/aeo-toolkit) (1★, 30+ skills / 7 agents, pushed 2026-06-14) | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API) | AEO / GEO | **enhanced-ours** | Lowest-starred and stalest source adopted from in weeks — and, as on 2026-08-03, star count was not the evidence. The find is its `entity-optimization` skill, whose organizing idea is that **being recognized is a precondition of being cited, and recognition is settled off your own site**: an entity home, `sameAs` as an identity join, knowledge-base presence, fact consistency across independent profiles, and disambiguation, treated as **one discipline** rather than as schema properties. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`sameAs`, `knowledge graph`, `Wikidata`, `disambiguat`, `entity home`, `Person schema`, `notability`, and `unlinked mention` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `Organization schema` appeared in exactly two agents, both times as a markup-checklist line; `About page` returned zero. So `seo-ai-search-optimizer` Rule 2 — "AI systems heavily weight entity signals for citation decisions" — asserted the mechanism and then sent the reader entirely to their own JSON-LD, and the Entity Markup Implementation Plan deliverable is on-site only. The agent claimed entity signals decide citations while owning only the half of the entity a company can write itself. Shipped as **"Recognition Before Citation: The Entity Lives Off Your Site"** + **Critical Rule 10** + an **Entity Recognition Audit & Reconciliation Plan** deliverable, in both dual-located copies. Ideas only, written from scratch. **Four layers the source does not have are ours:** (a) **fact drift as a dated-event problem** — the wrong description of a company is almost never invented, it is *the last true thing still being repeated*, so the reconciliation sweep attaches to the raise / rename / acquisition / leadership announcement (handed to `pmm-launch-manager`) rather than to a quarterly hygiene calendar; (b) the **recognized-and-wrong triage order** — a confident wrong description outranks an absence, because it propagates, reads as authoritative to a buyer, and gets quoted back to reps, while an absence merely costs a mention (four states, with a hedging engine counted **unknown**, never rounded up to recognized — the repo's standing discipline applied again); (c) the **brand-question vs. topic-question split** — the AI Share-of-Voice Heatmap shipped 07-26 samples *topic* questions and grades *presence*, which measures citability; recognition needs *brand* questions graded on the **description**, and the two were being conflated; (d) the **Wikipedia paid-editing guardrail** — the source lists Wikipedia as a lever with a notability caveat and stops, but the Wikimedia Terms of Use require paid contributors to disclose employer/client/affiliation and the COI guideline directs them to talk pages or Articles for Creation, so "get a Wikipedia page" is not a marketing task to assign and an undisclosed agency-written article is a worse outcome than no article. Also **ours**: the seam with `pmm-customer-advocacy` (it owns whether the reviews are good; this owns whether the profile describes the same company), and the explicit *no-transfer-function* honesty note — no engine documents how it resolves contradictory entity facts, so consistency is risk reduction under uncertainty, never a promised citation lift. **Two unmeasurable success metrics fixed in passing** (same call as `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30 and `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05): "Improve **Entity Authority scores** (measured through entity extract APIs) by 30-40%" names a score no entity-extraction API publishes, and "achieve **top 3 entity rankings** across 5-10 core topics" names a ranking that does not exist — both replaced with measurable statements (fact consistency against a canonical record; topic coverage read off the heatmap *with its sample size*), plus a new brand-question recognition metric. Every fact cited to a primary source read 2026-08-06: schema.org, Google's Organization structured-data guidance, Google's knowledge-panel help, Wikidata:Notability, Wikipedia:Paid-contribution disclosure. **No citation or ranking effect asserted for any action in the section.** |
| 2026-08-06 | [Thibaultbm/claude-seo-geo](https://github.com/Thibaultbm/claude-seo-geo) (13★, 18 skills, pushed 2026-08-03) | MIT | SEO / GEO | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | Second, independent sighting of the same thesis in the same run, which is what moved it from *interesting* to *worth a section*. Its `geo-visibility` skill defines an entity as "the machine-readable identity of a brand … that models learn from **co-occurrence across the web**" and scopes its own output to include "entity plus third-party consistency" — arriving at the off-site framing from a different direction (page-level citability) than the toolkit does (brand identity). Co-credited on the section; nothing textually adopted. The rest of the repo is page-type shaped (`seo-page-sections`' page-type matrix / section library / site archetypes, plus comparison, collection, product, and service-page skills) and is worth a look on a future content run — that is the same template-page ground as the still-open `seo-programmatic-strategist` proposal, and it also carries an Obsidian-vault read-first/write-back protocol adjacent to `brand-context.md`. |
| 2026-08-06 | [jstanx/aeo-toolkit](https://github.com/jstanx/aeo-toolkit) — `ai-referral-analytics` | MIT | AEO / measurement | **watch** | The same repo's second-best idea and a genuine gap. It makes the point that a large share of answer-engine traffic arrives **with no referrer at all** — native assistant apps, copy-pasted links, in-assistant browsers — and therefore lands in Direct, so the common read "we get almost no ChatGPT traffic" is frequently a measurement artifact rather than a finding. Gap grep-verified: **`chatgpt.com`, `utm_source=chatgpt`, and `AI referral` return zero hits repo-wide**; the two `referrer` hits are newsletter referral mechanics and the three `referral traffic` hits are all `seo-link-building-strategist` on link quality. Our AEO stack now measures *access* (07-30), *recognition* (today), and *presence in answers* (07-26) — and nothing measures **what arrives**, even though the Success Metrics already promise "attribute 5-10% of monthly qualified traffic to AI answer engine referrals." Not actioned — one change per run, and **the seam needs deciding first**: channel groupings, custom channel groups, and the observed/modeled/missing decomposition are `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` ground (07-27, 08-01), while the AEO interpretation is this agent's. Filed to backlog. |
| 2026-08-06 | [coffeegrind123/seo-manager](https://github.com/coffeegrind123/seo-manager) (0★, 24 reference workflows, pushed 2026-08-02) | MIT | SEO (measurement) | **watch** | Terminal-shaped SEO program (stdlib-Python, no backend) whose `workflow-geo-scan` independently reproduces the AI-crawler tiering we shipped 2026-07-30 — `ai_search` / `ai_user` / `ai_training`, with training crawlers explicitly marked as never citing — and adds one framing worth keeping: for a *user-triggered* fetch, "it already is" citing you, because a live fetch means a real person asked and the assistant came to the page. Its distinct un-owned idea is **index-bloat scoring** (`index bloat` returns zero hits repo-wide), which is squarely the still-open `seo-programmatic-strategist` proposal's territory rather than a standalone section, so it is recorded against that decision rather than filed as its own item. Also carries a "you are the instrument" rule for AI-visibility sampling with an explicit *never fabricate an answer from memory* guard — the same discipline as our honest-measurement note. |
| 2026-08-06 | [Auriti-Labs/geo-optimizer-skill](https://github.com/Auriti-Labs/geo-optimizer-skill) (645★, pushed 2026-08-06) | MIT | AEO / GEO | **dismissed** (mined, corroborates) | Mined twice already (citability rubric 07-21→07-26, crawler access 07-30) and the most actively developed AEO repo tracked. Checked its commits from today rather than its README: they are a corrected AI-bot roster against current vendor docs, a CDN/WAF live-fetch check limited to *citation-relevant* bots, and TTFB/page-weight surfacing — i.e. continued work on exactly the access layer we already own, including the same "re-verify the roster against vendor docs, operators change these often" instinct our section ends on. No entity/knowledge-graph coverage at all. Nothing new to adopt; useful as ongoing confirmation the access section is aimed correctly. |
| 2026-08-06 | [indranilbanerjee/contentforge](https://github.com/indranilbanerjee/contentforge) (20★, 22 skills / 13 agents, pushed 2026-08-01) | MIT | Content production | **watch** | New repo from a previously-assessed author (`digital-marketing-pro`, read in depth 08-01/08-03). An enterprise content-production pipeline — researcher → fact-checker → drafter → scientific-validator → structurer → SEO/GEO optimizer → humanizer → reviewer → output manager — with 11 quality gates, a 29-pattern AI humanizer, and C2PA-signed `.docx` output. Assessed from its skill and agent rosters, not read line by line. Two things put it on `watch` rather than dismissed: the **pipeline as a gated assembly line** (a distinct architecture from our per-deliverable content personas), and the fact that it is the second artifact from this author pointing at the still-open `ops-legal-compliance` proposal (C2PA provenance, EU AI Act Art. 50). Its `content-refresh` skill overlaps the decay triage shipped 08-02, and the humanizer/slop corner is held by `ops-quality-assurance` (AI-tell screen 07-26, reference class 07-31). |

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|

The source pulls competitors' live ads from the Meta and LinkedIn ad libraries and reads category-wide phrase-frequency patterns under a "working-material, never redistributed" discipline. Gap grep-verified — `ad library` / `ad-transparency` / `transparency center` / `creative velocity` / `working material` / `phrase-frequency` = **zero** across `product-marketing/`, `paid-media/`, `plugins/`; `pmm-competitive-intelligence` Rule 1 lists every independent CI source and omits advertising. **Owner: `pmm-competitive-intelligence`** (a new independent *source* feeding its existing profiles/battle-cards/dashboard); `paid-media-creative-strategist` has no competitor-ad coverage so no seam conflict, and the creative-*inspiration* use is handed to it by a one-line note. Shipped **Critical Rule 9** + a **"Reading Competitors' Live Advertising"** section + a **Competitor Ad-Activity Scan** deliverable + an **Advertising Signal Coverage** metric in both dual-located copies. The source's paid **Apify scraper is deliberately not adopted** — pointed instead at the three official, free, public libraries (LinkedIn Ad Library, Google Ads Transparency Center, Meta Ad Library), each cited to its primary docs read 2026-08-07. **Ours:** the three-official-library framing, the presence-is-a-claim-not-a-result rule, the absence-is-unknown rule, the creative-volume-as-a-state read, the appeared-vs-announced lead-time metric, and the Creative-Strategist seam. No competitor spend/impression/effectiveness figure asserted; lint 2/2, copies diff-identical. **`olson-adam/ad-mastermind` is now fully worked.** |

## 2026-08-07 — focus: paid media / social (13 sources → 1 enhance, 1 watch, 11 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: paid/social was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-03, the oldest of the four — PMM/sales/GTM ran 08-04, email/analytics/ops 08-05, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-06. Its queue also entered this run empty: the CPL-vs-CRM reconciliation item closed, the stale Meta Audience Network line in `phase-4-launch` fixed, and both halves of the `olson-adam/ad-mastermind` mine shipped (discrimination control 08-06, competitor-ad field scans 08-07). **This run took a gap no source in the tracked market owns**, rather than a source's idea — see the first row.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-08-07 | **Market-wide gap** — corroborated by [virajsutar-marketer/paid-ads-agent](https://github.com/virajsutar-marketer/paid-ads-agent) (0★, pushed 2026-08-06) and [Ad-Superpowers/ad-superpowers-plugin](https://github.com/Ad-Superpowers/ad-superpowers-plugin) `google-ads-display-campaign-optimizer` | MIT | Programmatic / display | **enhanced-ours** | The first enhancement this run took from an **absence** rather than from a source's idea, and the absence is the finding. Every paid collection tracked here was checked for programmatic supply coverage — `claude-ads`, `ad-superpowers-plugin`, `gmmh1/claude-paid-media-skills`, `hyperfx-ai/marketing-skills`, `rama-marketing` — and **none owns buy-side supply quality**. What they all call "display" is Google Display Network *campaign settings* (responsive display ads, placement targeting, audience lists), not DSP supply auditing. `virajsutar-marketer/paid-ads-agent`, a new MIT B2B kit, is the only source surveyed with a fraud/IVT section at all (§13) and it is four lines of search-side click-pattern watching with no supply-chain, MFA, or viewability content. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`ads.txt`, `sellers.json`, `supply chain`, `supply path`, `Active View`, `measurable impression`, `log-level`, `domain report`, `GIVT`, `SIVT`, `pre-bid`, `verification vendor`, `arbitrage`, `inclusion list`, `invalid traffic`, `click fraud`, `MFA site`, and `made for advertising` all returned **zero** hits repo-wide**; `viewab` returned exactly one, a blurb line in `paid-media-ops/SKILL.md` and in no agent; `premium publisher` and `allowlist` returned hits only inside `paid-media-programmatic-buyer`'s own Rule 6 and its Brand Safety deliverable — i.e. the control asserted, with no instrument. So the agent (61 lines, **never enhanced since the first pass** — one of two such paid agents) mandated "fraud detection" and a "premium publisher list" and supplied no method for either. Shipped as **"Buying an Audience, Receiving an Impression: Auditing Supply Quality"** + **Critical Rule 9** + a **Supply Quality & Domain Disposition Audit** deliverable, in both dual-located copies. **The organizing idea is ours and is the display-layer form of the thesis this repo has now applied three times** (LinkedIn delivery-vs-targeting 08-03, anomaly instrument-first 08-05): the buy is a *request for an audience*; what arrives is a *log of impressions on domains*. **Seven layers are ours:** (a) **the three-questions split** — brand safety (was my ad beside bad content), invalid traffic (was it a human), and supply quality (was the domain worth buying, through a chain worth paying, in a slot anyone could see) are three questions with three instruments, and Rule 6 collapsed them into one line, leaving the third with no instrument at all; (b) **the fraud number is someone else's log** — Google filters IVT pre-bid and credits it post-serve, and the "Invalid clicks" column reports traffic *already detected and already not charged*, so the agent's old `<2% fraudulent impression rate` metric measured the platform's filter rather than the buy, and is now replaced (platform IVT is a **floor under self-report**, not a proof, and SIVT is by the platform's own account the category its filter is worst at); (c) **the B2B cheap-CPM inversion** — a small budget against a narrow audience puts the DSP under pressure to find *any* matching inventory, and MFA inventory matches every segment because its audience was purchased, so on a hard-to-reach B2B audience a surprisingly cheap CPM is a *diagnostic*, not a win; (d) **the measurable/viewable pairing rule** — viewable rate is denominated in *measurable* impressions, not delivered ones, so a placement you mostly cannot see into reports a flattering viewable rate on the sliver you can, and **unmeasurable never rounds to viewable, it rounds to unknown** (the repo's standing discipline, applied again, with its own disposition row); (e) **the spend-sorted report and the concentration curve** — sort by spend, not impressions, because impression-ranked reports foreground cheap inventory, and read the *shape*: thousands of domains each taking a sliver is not reach, it is the DSP failing to find the audience and settling for whatever cleared the bid; (f) **log-level access is a contract term, not a setting** — so where the agreement does not grant it the honest state is *unverified*, never a passing control; (g) **the exclusion treadmill and the inversion** — MFA supply regenerates faster than a block list grows, so the structural fix is an inclusion list, which costs reach and raises CPMs, and that is the correct B2B trade because reach was never the binding constraint (this is the mechanism Rule 1's premium-inventory instinct was missing). Also ours: the **viewability-as-floor-not-goal** warning (50% of pixels for one second is *possible to see*, and the moment it becomes the KPI the cheapest way to hit it is to buy inventory that games it), the **PPC seam** (Google Ads placement exclusions and their documented limits are the PPC Strategist's ground — maintain **one** shared exclusion list, not two that drift), and the **precondition framing** (a clean supply audit on a campaign with no incrementality evidence is a well-run buy of *unknown value*, handing the question back to Rule 4). **Two unmeasurable success metrics fixed in passing** (same call as `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30, `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05, `seo-ai-search-optimizer` 08-06): the `<2% fraudulent impression rate` above, and "Achieve 85%+ of impressions on **premium publishers**" — a percentage against a publisher tier the file never defines — now replaced by the measurable-rate/viewable-rate pair plus inclusion-list spend share against the account's own baseline. Every fact quoted from and cited to a primary source read 2026-08-07: [DV360 — Filtering invalid traffic](https://support.google.com/displayvideo/answer/6076504) (pre-bid vs post-serve; GIVT vs SIVT), [Google Ads — About invalid traffic](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/11182074), [IAB Tech Lab — Using OpenRTB Signals to Identify MFA](https://iabtechlab.com/using-openrtb-signals-to-identify-made-for-advertising/) (MFA definition; identification as manual judgment on combined indicators; "the problem starts when you click on a headline"), [Google Ads — Active View / viewability](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/7029393) (50%/1s display, 30%/1s large ≥242,500px, 50%/2s video; the four Active View metrics), [IAB Tech Lab — ads.txt](https://iabtechlab.com/ads-txt/), [IAB Tech Lab — sellers.json & SupplyChain Object](https://iabtechlab.com/sellers-json/). **No MFA waste percentage is asserted** — published estimates vary widely by study, year, and SSP, and the ANA's own follow-up reported a large decline once buyers began excluding it, so the file instructs measuring your own domain report instead of importing a figure. Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; all 6 new URLs HTTP 200. |
| 2026-08-07 | [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (14★, **106 skills**, pushed 2026-07-30) | MIT | Organic social | **watch** | First sighting and the most structurally interesting organic-social repo surveyed — every skill ships a `SKILL.md` plus a `references/` set and several carry `evals/evals.json`, and each has a dated `<topic>-2026-reality.md` file separating *what is true now* from durable method. ## 2026-08-08 — focus: PMM / sales / GTM (16 sources → 1 enhance (+1 co-credit), 2 watch, 12 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM/sales/GTM was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-04, the oldest of the four — email/analytics/ops ran 08-05, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-06, paid/social 08-07. Its queue also entered this run empty: the `matteotitta/genesys-skills` segment-routing mine shipped 08-04 and the `Fearofsnakes/pmm-skillset` message-validation item shipped 08-05, leaving only the pricing SCOPE-QUESTION, which needs a maintainer decision rather than more evidence.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-08-08 | [stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills](https://github.com/stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills) (5★, 3 plugins / ~15 skills, pushed 2026-08-06) | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API; LICENSE reads "Copyright (c) 2026 Stefanos Karakasis") | PMM / launch | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting. A tight PMM-only set (`pre-mortem`, `retro`, `pmm-okrs`, `stakeholder-maps`, `prd`, `beachhead-segment`, `go-to-market-strategy`, `meta-learn`/`meta-review`), every skill shipping an `evals/` file. The find is `pre-mortem`, whose organizing idea is that risk work should be **structured as a deliverable with dispositions and owners**, not a worry list: a three-way classification (their Tigers / Paper Tigers / Elephants — deal-blocking, loud-but-manageable, knowingly accepted), a rule that every blocking risk carries a **named person plus the observable signal that would prove the risk real** ("not prevention, proof"), and a **go / conditional-go / hold** verdict tied to unmitigated blockers. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`pre-mortem`, `premortem`, `prospective hindsight`, `red team`, `falsif`, and `tripwire` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `risk register` existed only in `pm-client-success-manager` (agency delivery risk) and `ops-legal-compliance` (compliance risk), `kill criteri` only in `paid-media-budget-optimizer` (spend), and `postpone` **only** in the target agent's own Rule 3. So `pmm-launch-manager` — 67 lines, **never enhanced since the first pass**, one of the two smallest PMM agents — carried a complete apparatus for *schedule and completion* risk (Rule 2's 30% buffer, Rule 3's signed checklist, Rule 4's dependency reviews) and **nothing that asked whether the launch would work**; its single postponement trigger fired on an unchecked box. Shipped as **"Assume It Already Failed: The Launch Pre-Mortem"** + **Critical Rule 9** + a **Launch Pre-Mortem & Risk Register** deliverable + two success metrics, in both dual-located copies. Ideas only, written from scratch. **Six layers the source does not have are ours:** (a) the **before-or-after-launch split on every observable** — the source requires signals be "measurable and observable" but never asks *when they resolve*, and a signal that resolves 30 days after the irreversible act cannot inform the go decision, so blocking risks divide into pre-launch-*testable* (not a risk at all — an unfinished task, routed to the Rule 4 dependency review with a date) and post-launch-only (a **tripwire**: threshold, watcher, cadence, and the prepared response written *now*, because nobody designs a good response in week two of a bad launch), with the two counts reported separately since an all-post-launch register reads rigorous and changed no decision; (b) the **run-before-sign-off ordering rule** — after Rule 3's sign-off every function head has put their name to "ready," so raising a blocker becomes an accusation and a self-reversal, and the person accountable for the date must not facilitate, since that seats the strongest incentive-for-everything-to-be-manageable in the adjudicating chair; (c) the **sales-cycle horizon** — the source's generic "3 months out" is launch-shaped, but B2B SaaS launch failures resolve on the *deal* clock (sales stops pitching by week three; it is demoed and never becomes a reason to buy; the champion can't re-explain it internally; the analyst brief lands and no shortlist changes), so the question is asked at one full *measured* average cycle past launch; (d) **acceptance expires** — an accepted trade-off is accepted *under a condition* ("no SSO, we aren't selling enterprise this year"), which silently stops holding the quarter the segment changes, so each one records its condition and the next launch's pre-mortem opens by re-reading them; (e) the **instrument routing table**, the repo-native move — a pre-mortem names risks and picks instruments rather than inventing tests, and six of the standard failure narratives already have an owner here (message validation → `pmm-messaging-architect` 08-05; reps pitching back cold → `sales-enablement-content-creator`/`sales-discovery-coach`; competitor pre-empt → the ad-library scan shipped to `pmm-competitive-intelligence` 08-07; claim substantiation → the Proof Point Library; segment proof → `pmm-customer-advocacy`; measurability-before-launch → the two analytics agents); (f) the **zero-blocking-risks-is-a-failed-exercise rule** — a pre-mortem *elicits*, it does not predict, so its yield is a property of who was in the room and how cheap dissent was there, which is the discrimination-control discipline shipped to `ops-quality-assurance` 08-06 applied to risk elicitation, plus an after-the-fact calibration log that records **which real failures nobody named**, not only which predicted risks materialized. **One metric fixed in passing** (same call as `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30, `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05, `seo-ai-search-optimizer` 08-06, `paid-media-programmatic-buyer` 08-07): "Launch Readiness Score — 100% of function teams sign off … zero 'not ready' items" is the exact metric the new section says cannot bear the weight put on it, and is now explicitly labeled a *completion* measure to be paired with the two pre-mortem metrics rather than read as confidence. Technique attributed to **Gary Klein, [Performing a Project Premortem](https://hbr.org/2007/09/performing-a-project-premortem), HBR September 2007** (fetched and verified this run, including the "makes it safe for dissenters … to speak up" rationale the ordering rule rests on); research lineage cited to **Mitchell, Russo & Pennington 1989**, *Journal of Behavioral Decision Making* 2(1) 25–38 ([doi:10.1002/bdm.3960020103](https://doi.org/10.1002/bdm.3960020103), resolves 302 → Wiley). **The widely-circulated "~30% better at identifying reasons for future outcomes" figure was deliberately NOT asserted** — that paper's own reported result is that outcome *uncertainty* strongly shaped explanations while temporal perspective did little, so the popular figure is contested against its own source; the file says so explicitly and rests the technique on what is defensible. No launch-outcome or risk-detection figure asserted anywhere in the section. Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; 268 internal links, 0 broken; 3 of 4 new external URLs HTTP 200 and the fourth (the DOI) verified resolving. |
