## 2026-08-09 — recovery run (not a scout survey): committing and completing an interrupted 08-09 enhancement

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-09 | [stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills](https://github.com/stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills) — `pmm-execution/skills/stakeholder-maps` | MIT (re-verified this run via the GitHub license API; repo live, 5★, pushed 2026-08-06) | PMM / launch politics | **enhanced-ours** | Closes the `watch` row filed 2026-08-08 and the matching backlog item. **Third distinct idea mined from this repo** (after `pre-mortem` and `retro`), which is itself the finding: it is the most productive single source in the ledger. Its thesis — a launch is killed from *inside* at least as often as by the market, and an org chart is not a political map — lands on `pmm-launch-manager`'s pre-mortem as a new failure family rather than as a standalone exercise, because the pre-mortem is the one moment already designed to make private doubt sayable. Read in full this run rather than trusted from the interrupted run's notes: it does carry champion/blocker/frozen/gatekeeper roles, a Power × Interest grid, and an explicit *silent blocker scan*, so the attribution's characterization is accurate. Gap re-verified against our own files before committing: `sales-deal-strategist` maps the **customer's** buying committee (economic buyer, blocker, champion — thread coverage shipped 07-28), `pm-campaign-coordinator`'s stakeholder map is a **communications** plan (role, frequency, method, escalation), and `pm-client-success-manager`'s risk register is **agency↔client** relationship risk. None reads an internal stakeholder's *stance toward the launch itself*. **Ideas-only, written from scratch:** the fund-it / staff-it / defend-it test, the sign-off-versus-alignment distinction, the silent-doubter stance taxonomy, the seams with the two agents above, and the refusal to express the read as a percent-aligned figure (that number is what the Rule 3 sign-off already produces, and going behind it is the point) are ours. Their skill's session-logging and meta-pattern machinery was **not** taken — it presumes a persistent `/context/` brain this repo does not have. |

## 2026-08-10 — focus: email / analytics / marketing-ops (scout survey → 1 enhance)

Discipline chosen by rotation: email/analytics/ops was last the *focus* on 2026-08-05 (content/SEO ran 08-06, paid/social 08-07, PMM/sales/GTM 08-08; 08-09 was a recovery + AEO/GEO freshness run, not a survey), the oldest of the four. Its queue entered this run effectively empty — `growth-accounting` shipped 08-06, and the two remaining watch items (`tracking-plan` instrumentation contract, `pricing-and-packaging`) are open SCOPE-QUESTIONs awaiting a maintainer decision rather than more evidence.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-10 | [phuryn/pm-skills](https://github.com/phuryn/pm-skills) (25,028★, 68 skills / 9 plugins, pushed 2026-07-03) — `pm-data-analytics/ab-test-analysis` | MIT (verified via GitHub license API) | Analytics / experimentation (CRO) | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting. A large, well-starred **product-management** collection — most of it (discovery, strategy, execution, PRDs) is outside our marketing scope — but its `ab-test-analysis` skill owns the one thing our `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer` did not: **experiment *trustworthiness*, as distinct from experiment *power*.** Read the actual SKILL.md via `gh api` (not description) before crediting — it runs a result through a randomization/**SRM** check, a **novelty/primacy** wash-out check, **guardrail metrics** (revenue/engagement/page-load a winner must not degrade), a two-tailed z/chi-squared significance test, and a **ship / investigate / extend / stop / don't-ship** matrix. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`sample ratio mismatch`, `SRM`, `optional stopping`, `novelty effect`, `primacy effect` = zero hits repo-wide**; `guardrail metric` only on `social-twitter-strategist`; `peeking` only on `paid-media-creative-strategist` + one line in `marketing-analytics/SKILL.md`. So the CRO agent (72 lines, **never enhanced since the first pass**) had the *power* half in Rule 2 (sample size, 95%) and **nothing on trust** — and its "reach 95% before declaring a winner" actively *invites* peeking. This is the same pattern the ledger keeps recording (LinkedIn delivery 08-03, content decay 08-02, entity recognition 08-06, supply quality 08-07, launch risk 08-08): the discipline is owned in an **adjacent** collection and absent from our marketing persona. Shipped as **Critical Rule 9** + a **"Trust the Split Before the Winner: When a Significant Result Is Still an Artifact"** section + an **Experiment Trust Report** deliverable + a Rule 2 pointer, in both dual-located copies. **The organizing frame is ours — power asks *could I detect it*, trust asks *did the machinery produce the number I'm reading*, and trust runs *before* the verdict** — and **four layers the source does not have are ours:** (a) the **B2B small-N inversion** — the SRM check is itself a statistical test, so at the few-hundred-conversion volumes a B2B page produces a genuinely broken 53/47 split can pass as "no SRM," making a *passing* SRM check weak evidence, not a clean bill of health, and mandating a *direct* look at the assignment mechanism (redirect symmetry, per-arm latency, uneven bot filtering, assignment stickiness) — the trust-check twin of the paid strategist's underpowered-primary-test point; (b) the **discard-not-adjust rule** — an SRM whose cause you cannot find is thrown away, never reweighted back to 50/50, because a randomizer you cannot trust to split evenly you cannot trust to have split *representatively* (the repo's standing "unknown never rounds to pass," applied to assignment, with Twyman's law given a mechanism); (c) the **guardrail generalization** — Rule 4 already refuses a form-fill lift that lowers lead quality, extended here to a declared guardrail set (unsubscribe/complaint on email tests, page-load and client error on template changes, the downstream SQL/pipeline chain); (d) the **two seams** — *power for paid creative tests* stays with `paid-media-creative-strategist` (pre-registered MDE, four outcome states, 07-30) and *observational anomaly* reads stay with `analytics-performance-analyst` (measurement-issue-first, 08-05), and this section owns only the layer between them, believing the result of an on-site experiment you *did* randomize. **One success metric fixed in passing** (same call as `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30, `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05, `seo-ai-search-optimizer` 08-06, `paid-media-programmatic-buyer` 08-07): "60-70% of tests show positive results" made *winning* a quota — the exact anti-pattern peeking exploits — now replaced by a trust-gate-pass rate, with a 60-70% *win* rate flagged as itself a symptom of peeking or under-powering. Ideas-only, written from scratch; SRM as a chi-squared goodness-of-fit flagged at a strict threshold and its cause taxonomy cited to **Kohavi, Tang & Xu, *Trustworthy Online Controlled Experiments* (Cambridge University Press, 2020)** and **Fabijan et al., "Diagnosing Sample Ratio Mismatch in Online Controlled Experiments" (KDD 2019)**. No conversion-lift or error-rate figures asserted. Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; phuryn/pm-skills HTTP 200. |

## 2026-08-10 (second run) — focus: content / SEO-AEO-GEO (11 sources → 1 enhance, 2 watch, 8 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: content/SEO-AEO-GEO was last the *focus* on 2026-08-06, the oldest of the four (paid/social 08-07, PMM/sales/GTM 08-08, email/analytics/ops earlier today; 08-09 was a recovery + AEO/GEO freshness run, not a survey). Its queue entered this run holding only the `seo-programmatic-strategist` PROPOSAL, blocked on a maintainer scope decision rather than more evidence.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-10 | [kostja94/marketing-skills](https://github.com/kostja94/marketing-skills) (875★, pushed 2026-06-09) — `skills/seo/technical/indexing` | MIT (re-verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Technical SEO / indexation | **enhanced-ours** | **Re-opened a source dismissed 2026-07-21** — that dismissal was of its *social* skills ("formulaic hook/viral tactics, off-brand for B2B"), and its technical-SEO corner is different ground entirely. Its `indexing` skill treats the Search Console not-indexed states as **distinct causes with distinct fixes** rather than as one crawl problem, and carries the robots.txt-versus-noindex interaction and a removal-mechanism decision table. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`not indexed`, `Page indexing`, `URL Inspection`, `X-Robots-Tag`, `nofollow`, `noindex,follow`, `redirect chain`, `410`, and `Removals` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `soft 404` appeared exactly twice and both were the *same* promise — a `seo-technical-auditor` deliverable naming "soft 404 diagnosis" with no method behind it. So the agent (**52 lines, the smallest in the repo, never enhanced since the first pass**) modeled indexing as a plumbing outcome: every lever it holds (robots, canonicals, sitemaps, crawl budget, rendering) governs *eligibility*, and it had no branch for the state Google documents as `Crawled - currently not indexed` — fetched cleanly, read, and declined — against which all of them are no-ops. Its Success Metric nonetheless promised "95%+ of target pages indexed within 30 days," a number the file gave no instrument for. Shipped **Critical Rule 9** + an **"Indexed Is a Decision, Not a Delivery"** section + an **Index Disposition Audit** deliverable + a rewritten metric, in both dual-located copies. **Six layers are ours:** (a) the **four-disposition model** — intended exclusion / broken signal / deferred crawl / declined, mapping each Search Console state to an *owner* rather than counting one "pages not indexed" total, because a healthy B2B SaaS site excludes login, thank-you, internal-search, filter, staging, and gated-asset URLs on purpose and an audit that drives that number toward zero produces work that makes the site worse; (b) **deferred is a volume question before it is a server question** — Google's stated cause for `Discovered - currently not indexed` is crawl scheduling against site load, which is literal on a large catalog and *not established* on a few-thousand-URL B2B site where the server plainly isn't straining, so the honest output is "cause unknown, here is the URL inventory," never a server-upgrade recommendation; (c) **route don't fix** — the declined bucket is a content-selection outcome with no technical lever, so it is handed to `seo-content-optimizer` (decay triage / cannibalization / the merge-canonical-differentiate-retire dispositions) and `content-blog-strategist` (page-class-at-scale) with the technical field explicitly cleared first, which is the **return leg** of a handoff that previously ran one way only (`seo-content-optimizer`'s impostor check already sends indexation questions here); (d) the **Request-Indexing reflex named as the waste it is**, on Google's own wording that there is "no need to resubmit this URL for crawling"; (e) the **gated-PDF `X-Robots-Tag` case** — the assets a B2B SaaS company least wants indexed have no `<head>`, and an indexed gated whitepaper is a demand-gen failure long before anyone reads it as a crawling one; (f) the **intended-exclusion map declared before the audit**, so intentional exclusions stop being rediscovered as findings every cycle, plus **unknown never rounds to indexed** (the repo's standing discipline: the Page indexing report is lagged and sampled, URL Inspection answers one URL at a time, and a URL you have not resolved is unseen, not clean). **The source's 404-vs-410 distinction was deliberately NOT adopted as stated** — Google's status-code documentation says all `4xx` except `429` are treated the same, so that row was rebuilt from primary docs and `410` is presented as a signal to humans and other systems, never as a faster de-index lever (same call as the GPTBot tiering on 07-30, where a source's classification contradicted vendor documentation). **Two staleness bugs fixed in passing:** `INP` was **zero** repo-wide while `FID`/`First Input Delay` appeared in five places — the repo taught a Core Web Vital Google retired **2024-09-09** (INP replaced it 2024-03-12) with a threshold nobody measures — corrected across the agent (both copies + a dated note), `seo-growth/SKILL.md`, and `phase-0-discovery` + its twin; and Rule 6's **Mobile-Friendly Test** (sunset 2023-12-01 with its API and the Mobile Usability report) became the URL Inspection tool's rendered-HTML view, with "GSC Index Coverage report" corrected to its current name, the **Page indexing** report. Ideas-only, written from scratch. All states, definitions, directive behavior, and status-code claims quoted from and cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-10 ([Page indexing report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/7440203), [Block search indexing with noindex](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/block-indexing), [HTTP status codes and network errors](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/http-network-errors), [Remove information from Google](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/remove-information)); CWV dates/thresholds to [web.dev](https://web.dev/articles/inp) + the [INP launch post](https://web.dev/blog/inp-cwv-launch). No indexation-rate, ranking, or recovery-time figure asserted. Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; 286 internal links, 0 broken. |

## 2026-08-11 — focus: paid media / social (14 sources → 1 enhance (+2 co-credit), 2 watch, 11 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: paid/social was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-07, the oldest of the four — PMM/sales/GTM ran 08-08, email/analytics/ops 08-10, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-10 (second run). Its queue entered this run effectively empty: the `olson-adam/ad-mastermind` mine closed 08-07, the CPL-vs-CRM item closed 08-03, and the `social-media-skills/skills` watch item has had three of its four slices worked (LinkedIn company pages 08-07, experimentation 08-08, crisis/moderation 08-09), leaving only its freshness-convention *architecture* question, which is a maintainer call rather than a gap.

| 2026-08-11 | [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (2,545★, 120 skills, pushed 2026-08-11) — `ad/scale/budget-pacing-monitor` | Apache-2.0 (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Paid / budget delivery | **enhanced-ours** | **Re-opened a source on `watch` since 2026-07-30** — that watch was filed against its *email* corpus (the opt-down ladder, shipped 07-30); its **ad** corner had never been mined. Its `budget-pacing-monitor` is the sharpest single statement in the market that **pacing is a discipline distinct from allocation**, with an explicit seam back to its own `budget-optimizer`: fix the target curve (even / front-loaded / back-loaded) *before* reading spend, confirm learning-phase status before acting, compute percent-to-pace against the curve rather than a bare percentage, call over/under-delivery **with a named driver** (cap-limited, bid-throttled, low-volume, dayparting), and separate the observed gap from its plausible cause. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`underdeliver`, `under-deliver`, `overdeliver`, `over-deliver`, `budget cap`, `shared budget`, `burn rate`, `flighting`, `learning phase`, `budget constrained`, and `spend delivery` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `pacing` in the paid sense resolved to exactly three real hits — a *frequency*-pacing line in `paid-media-programmatic-buyer` (a different discipline) and two blurb bullets in `paid-media-ops/SKILL.md` ("spend pacing vs. budget" in a weekly-metrics list, and a dashboard line). The pattern the ledger keeps recording, again: **the control named in a metrics list, with no instrument and no owner.** So `paid-media-budget-optimizer` — **61 lines, the thinnest paid agent and never enhanced since the first pass**, the last of the two flagged on 08-07 — mandated empirical diminishing-returns curves (Rule 2), quarterly reoptimization (Rule 5), and scenario forecasts (Rule 3), every one of them fit over *delivered* spend, and had no way to ask whether the plan it modeled was ever executed. Shipped **Critical Rule 9** + a **"Planned Is Not Delivered: Reading Pacing Before the Curve"** section + a **Budget Delivery & Pacing Report** deliverable + three rewritten metrics, in both dual-located copies. **The organizing idea is ours and it is a validity precondition, not ops hygiene:** raise a budget and watch returns flatten — that is what saturation looks like, and it is exactly what a **delivery constraint** looks like, because money a channel cannot absorb returns nothing either; the chart cannot tell them apart and only the delivery record can, so **a saturation threshold observed at a spend level the channel never exceeded is the top of the observed range**, and under-delivery rounds to *unknown above here*, never to saturated (the repo's standing "unknown never rounds to pass," applied to a curve). **Five further layers are ours:** (a) the **B2B inversion** — the market's pacing writing is consumer-shaped and worries about burning the day's budget by noon, while in B2B the binding constraint is the *audience*, so the channel under plan is the ordinary case and a channel at 70% of plan quarter after quarter has a reach ceiling needing reallocation, not a bid increase; (b) **a ceiling is not a plan** (the kernel taken from `logly/mureo`, credited) and **the daily number is an average, not a cap**, so a single heavy day is not an incident and the read belongs at the month-to-date or flight level; (c) **underspend is not saved money, it is an unplanned reallocation** with three destinations that all rewrite the allocation this agent designed — expired at close, pooled by a shared budget to the broadest campaign that *can* absorb it (so the platform's reallocation runs systematically opposite to a precision B2B allocation), or spent in a period-end catch-up that buys the worst inventory of the period at its worst price; (d) **"Limited by budget" is a recommendation with an interest** — Google documents it as firing at "5% or more of your potential traffic" and documents raising the budget as the remedy, and potential traffic is not qualified traffic, so it is an input and never the verdict; (e) the **three seams** — *authorization* for any budget move already belongs to `paid-media-ppc-strategist`'s spend-change gate (07-26/08-07) and this section supplies only the *reason*, flight-level DSP pacing (insertion-order totals, even/ahead modes) stays with `paid-media-programmatic-buyer`, and a perfectly-paced channel with no incrementality evidence is a well-delivered buy of *unknown value*, handed back to Rule 4 and `paid-media-attribution-analyst` (the same precondition framing as 08-07). **Two fabricated-precision success metrics fixed in passing** (same call as `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30, `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05, `seo-ai-search-optimizer` 08-06, `paid-media-programmatic-buyer` 08-07, `pmm-launch-manager` 08-08, `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer` 08-10): ">85% accuracy in scenario revenue forecasts" and "diminishing returns thresholds for 80%+ of channels with <10% variance vs. modeled results" — both of which grade the model's confidence in numbers the new section says the model has no right to, replaced by a curve-validity metric (zero saturation thresholds asserted above a channel's delivered range), a plan-vs-delivered reconciliation metric, and an in-period pacing cadence. Ideas-only, written from scratch; Apache-2.0 notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation and none was made. Every platform figure quoted from and cited to a primary source read 2026-08-11: [Google Ads — How Google Ads works with your budget](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/2375423) ("up to twice your average daily budget"; "no more than 30.4 times"), [Google Ads — About budget pacing insights](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/13685469) ("missing out on 5% or more of your potential traffic"), [Google Ads — About shared budgets](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/10487241) (automatic reallocation to budget-capped campaigns), [LinkedIn — Campaign and ad set budgets](https://www.linkedin.com/help/lms/answer/a422101) ("up to 50% higher than the daily budget amount on a given day"), [Meta Marketing API — Budgets](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/marketing-api/bidding/overview/budgets) ("up to 25% more than your daily budget may be spent"). **No under-delivery rate, waste percentage, or reallocation-uplift figure asserted.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; 296 internal links, 0 broken; all new external URLs verified live. |

Three ideas co-credited: **a guardrail is a ceiling, not a target** — deriving a monthly plan by multiplying a max-daily-budget produces a plan nobody wrote (adopted as "a ceiling is not a plan"); **exclude today from the run rate**, because a partial day biases the projection downward; and **early-period projections are low-confidence** (its threshold: fewer than 5 elapsed days), so they are a watch item rather than a decision. Its staged-change rule for avoiding a learning reset corroborates the same point from aaron. Nothing textually adopted — the skill body is MCP-wired execution over its own STRATEGY.md/STATE.json workspace, the architecture this repo has consistently found nothing adoptable in; Apache-2.0 notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation and none was made. |

Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM/sales/GTM was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-08 (with the 08-09 recovery run closing its stakeholder-map item), the oldest of the four — email/analytics/ops and content/SEO-AEO-GEO both ran 08-10, paid/social 08-11. Its queue entered this run holding only two open **SCOPE QUESTIONs** awaiting a maintainer decision (pricing/packaging ownership, five sightings; the grade-the-function persona) and the claim-provenance item, which carries the same advisory-vs-executable architecture caveat declined a dozen times.

| 2026-08-12 | [lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth](https://github.com/lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth) (1★, 14 agents + 1 orchestrator, pushed 2026-08-10) — `skills/customer-advocacy-references` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | PMM / customer marketing | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting, and the sharpest statement of the run's thesis despite the star count: **"advocates are not a renewable resource."** Its advocacy specialist routes every request `AE → Customer Marketing → CSM → advocate, never direct`, caps requests per advocate (its default: 1/quarter), treats **burnout as a revoked status** rather than a complaint, and runs a quarterly health check that flags *over-requested* and *under-utilized* as two failure directions off one roster. Its tiering is by what the advocate has consented to be cited as (publicly / privately / not for marketing), not by enthusiasm. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`advocate fatigue`, `over-request`, `request cap`, `cap requests`, `depletable`, `reference fatigue`, and `shared resource` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `burnout` resolved only to *internal team capacity* in `pm-resource-allocator` / `pm-client-success-manager`; `CSM` appeared repo-wide only in `pmm-launch-manager` and `customer success manager` nowhere; `logo rights`, `quote approval`, and `approval to publish` were zero, with the single adjacent line being `content-case-study-producer`'s per-asset "permission to publish" for one asset. So `pmm-customer-advocacy` — **71 lines, never enhanced since the first pass, the smallest PMM agent** — modeled advocacy as a *recruitment and inventory* problem end to end: Rule 5 sets a roster target, Rule 6 grades account health by churn risk, Rule 2's friction discipline governs the size of *one* ask and never the sum of them, and Rule 4's lifecycle timing is a program-side calendar with no relationship-side veto. **The finding that made this worth the run is in our own metrics:** "40-60 reference calls per quarter" against "5-10 actively available" advocates is **4-12 asks per person per quarter**, several times any published practitioner cap — the agent stated the roster-consuming quota as a *goal*. Shipped **Critical Rule 9** + a **"Budgeting the Ask: Advocate Capacity and Depletion"** section + an **Advocate Capacity Plan & Ask Ledger** deliverable + four rewritten/added success metrics, in both dual-located copies. **Eight layers are ours:** (a) the **capacity arithmetic** — program capacity is the sum of individuals' weighted ask budgets, computed *before* a volume target is published, with the old metric named in-file as the worked example of the failure; (b) the **per-person rather than per-account ledger** — the account is referenceable, a named human takes the call, and an account-level roster structurally hides that three requesters landed on one inbox; (c) **ask weighting** — a logo permission, a 30-minute call, a filmed case study, and a keynote are different costs, so a cap counted in *asks* invites substituting heavy ones for light ones, and the real time cost belongs *inside* the ask (Rule 2's friction discipline done honestly); (d) the **champion-job-change over-count** — the advocate is a person, so when they leave the account stays a customer and the roster silently keeps counting a reference that walked, with re-qualification of the successor required and the seam to `sales-outbound-strategist`'s job-change triggers drawn so the same event isn't double-used; (e) **recorded states, not silences** (available / unavailable-with-return-date / suppressed-with-reason / retired-with-cause), because an advocate quietly dropped still inflates the roster and the capacity plan keeps promising calls nobody will take — the repo's standing **unknown never rounds to available**, applied to a person; (f) the **latency tell** — depletion presents as slower replies and rescheduling, not refusal; (g) the **recipient's-policy framing of compensation** — the binding constraint on a thank-you is the customer's own employer gift/ethics rules (enterprise procurement, public sector, financial services, healthcare), which makes a cash-equivalent card a problem *for them* they are rarely free to explain, so the offer is a menu (charity donation, company credit, recognition, access) built to route around the individual-gift question; (h) the **two-direction read** — ask concentration as the fragility signal, advocates recruited-and-never-asked as the opposite failure, both off the same ledger and neither visible from a roster count. **The sources' per-quarter cap numbers (1, or 2-3) were deliberately NOT adopted as a rule** — they are practitioner defaults with no measurement behind them, and the file instructs setting and recording the cap rather than inheriting a number. **Rule 3 corrected in passing:** it prescribed a flat cash schedule ($50-100 gift cards for reference calls, $500-1000 case studies, $1000-2000 CAB) one rule away from Rule 7's "never incentivize reviews," with no line drawn between compensating a *reference call* and compensating a *public review*; rewritten to scale-by-effort under the recipient's rules, with the review case routed to Rule 7 and the platform's own policy. Ideas-only, written from scratch. G2's incentive policy quoted and cited to [G2's Community Guidelines](https://legal.g2.com/community-guidelines) (read 2026-08-12): the $100 USD cap, the incentivized label, that "eligibility to receive an incentive is never based on the opinions, positive or negative, expressed in the review," and the disclose-to-G2 requirement. The gift-policy reasoning is explicitly labeled in-file as our judgment, not a measured finding. **No burnout rate, decline rate, or advocacy-attributed revenue figure asserted.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; all four new external URLs HTTP 200. |

## 2026-08-13 — focus: email / analytics / marketing-ops (13 sources → 1 enhance (+1 co-credit), 2 watch, 9 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: email/analytics/ops was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-10 (first run), the oldest of the four — content/SEO-AEO-GEO ran 08-10 (second run), paid/social 08-11, PMM/sales/GTM 08-12. Its queue entered this run holding one open **SCOPE QUESTION** (who owns the instrumentation *contract* / tracking plan), which needs a maintainer decision rather than more evidence.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-13 | [justinwilliames/orbit-for-claude](https://github.com/justinwilliames/orbit-for-claude) (0★, 60+ skills, created 2026-03-31, pushed 2026-08-13) — `skills/apple-mpp-response` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Email / lifecycle measurement | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting, and the star count is the least interesting thing about it: a lifecycle-marketing OS (Braze/Iterable/Klaviyo/SFMC-shaped) whose `apple-mpp-response` skill is a single-purpose statement that **the open-rate metric changed meaning and nobody renamed it**. Read in full before crediting — it carries the mechanism (proxy prefetch regardless of engagement, timestamp reflects the fetch not the read, geolocation is Apple-proxy), a what-to-use-instead list, and an explicit refusal to recommend pixel workarounds. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`Mail Privacy Protection`, `MPP`, `tracking pixel`, `Litmus`, `preheader`, and `ESP migration` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `open rate` returned hits in **six** agents, all of them decision-bearing. So `email-deliverability-specialist` — **244 lines, never enhanced since the first release**, the largest email agent — selected IP-warming cohorts from "email openers from past 7 days," identified inactive contacts by "no opens/clicks in 180-365 days," defined `Engagement rate (opens + clicks / total delivered)`, set a `>25% engagement rate overall` success metric, and diagnosed "low engagement rate" as a content-quality issue with no branch for *the instrument changed*. The same pattern the ledger keeps recording (CRO trust 08-10, indexation disposition 08-10, budget pacing 08-11, advocate capacity 08-12): a load-bearing control consumed everywhere and owned nowhere. Shipped **Critical Rule 9** + a **"The Engagement Signal Is Machine-Contaminated"** section + an **Engagement Signal Integrity Audit** deliverable + three rewritten/added success metrics, in both dual-located copies. **Seven layers are ours:** (a) the **B2B click inversion** — the market's advice (this source included: "Click rate — reliable. Clicks require a real user interaction") is consumer-shaped and *inverts* in B2B, because corporate security gateways fetch and detonate links before delivery, so contamination scales with the security maturity of the account and the better the logo the dirtier the click; (b) the **asymmetry of the two failure modes** — a machine open is a false positive that quietly keeps a dead address on the list (the decay Rule 5 exists to prevent), while a machine click *fires machinery*, advancing nurture branches and tripping MQL thresholds, so the click consequence escapes this agent's blast radius and is handed explicitly to `email-automation-engineer` and `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`; (c) the **four-tier evidence model** (confirmed-human / probable-human / unconfirmed / silent) with the minimum tier each list decision may use, carrying the repo's standing **unknown never rounds to engaged** into the list — an address the machines have opened for eighteen months has produced no evidence a human exists behind it; (d) the **warming-cohort trap**, the highest-consequence line in the file and found in it — seeding a new IP from an opens-defined segment hands the ramp a cohort assembled by proxies at the exact moment providers form their first impression, so the rule is *extend the ramp rather than dilute the cohort*; (e) the **unsubscribe-`GET` consequence** — a scanner fetches the opt-out link too, so a bare `GET` unsubscribe lets a security gateway remove a live subscriber and the churn reads as content fatigue, with the seam to `email-lifecycle-architect`'s opt-down ladder (07-30) which shares the suppression plumbing; (f) the **instrument declaration** — a bot-filtering toggle and a placement incident produce the same step change in the chart, so no engagement figure ships without its platform, filtering posture, and date-of-last-change, and no filtered number is compared to an unfiltered benchmark or to pre-toggle history; (g) **naming what is still clean** (complaints, bounces, delivery rate, seed-list placement, `POST`-confirmed unsubscribes) so the section does not overcorrect into paralysis, plus opens retained as an **anomaly detector** since a proxy cannot fetch a pixel in a message that never arrived. **The source's prevalence figure (25-40% of subscribers) was deliberately NOT adopted** — it is explicitly consumer/DTC-derived and the source's own aside that "B2B skews lower — corporate inboxes often disable MPP" conflates the mail *provider* with the mail *client*, since a work account read in Apple Mail on an iPhone is fully affected; the file instructs measuring your own share by three own-data reads (Apple/non-Apple client split, delivery-to-open latency distribution, link-fanout shape) instead of inheriting a number. Ideas-only, written from scratch. Every mechanism quoted from and cited to a primary vendor or standards source read 2026-08-13: [Apple, *Mail Privacy Protection & Privacy*](https://www.apple.com/legal/privacy/data/en/mail-privacy-protection/) ("downloads remote content in the background by default — regardless of whether you engage with the email"; the two-relay IP split), [Microsoft Learn, *Safe Links overview*](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/defender-office-365/safe-links-about) ("URLs are scanned prior to message delivery, regardless of whether the URLs are rewritten or not"; URLs without a valid reputation "are detonated asynchronously in the background"), [HubSpot](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/marketing-email/understand-bot-filtering-in-marketing-email-analytics) ("privacy filters such as Apple's Mail Privacy Protection and corporate screeners such as Mimecast"; bot filtering "will usually show lower overall performance metrics"), [Klaviyo](https://help.klaviyo.com/hc/en-us/articles/22981852783899) ("Inbox providers, some 3rd-party security software, and carriers use bots to click links in emails before any human user"), [RFC 8058](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8058.html). **No prevalence, inflation, scanner-share, or engagement-lift figure asserted.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; 341 internal links, 0 broken; all 7 new external URLs HTTP 200. |
| 2026-08-13 | [Mailneo/skills](https://github.com/Mailneo/skills) (1★, MIT, pushed 2026-08-04) — `skills/email-lifecycle` (`SKILL.md` + `references/cadence-and-testing.md`) | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Email / lifecycle | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | Second independent statement of the same thesis, which is what moved it from one repo's idea to a discipline worth a section. Vendor-bound at the top level (its `skills/mailneo/` tree is an API operating manual, the shape dismissed for Sequenzy 07-30, Cogny 07-25, OpenRush 08-10), but its four *knowledge* skills are provider-neutral and the lifecycle one is disciplined: "Steer by clicks and conversions, not opens," benchmark tables shipped with the caveat that post-2021 numbers "are not comparable to older ones," and CTOR flagged as unreliable because its denominator is inflated. **It also disagrees with `orbit-for-claude` on the sharpest practical question, and resolving that disagreement produced our own layer:** orbit says a subject-line A/B test decided on opens is measuring proxy noise; Mailneo says opens remain usable for *relative* tests because "machine opens inflate both arms roughly equally." Both are half right — a roughly constant term added to both arms rarely reverses a winner, but it enlarges the denominator while carrying none of the effect, which **shrinks the observed lift and drains the test's power**, so a test sized against the contaminated rate is systematically under-powered and "no significant winner" is the expected result even when a real one exists. Shipped as the testing paragraph with the seams drawn to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer` (experiment trust, 08-10) and `email-copywriter`. Ideas only; nothing textually adopted. |
| 2026-08-13 | [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (2,574★, pushed 2026-08-13) — the `email/` corpus (`deliver/inbox-placement-monitor`, `deliver/send-experiment-designer`, `deliver/email-quality-auditor`) | Apache-2.0 | Email / deliverability | **dismissed** (no coverage) — *corroborates the gap* | The largest permissively-licensed email corpus in the ledger, on `watch` for its email tree since 2026-07-30 (the opt-down ladder shipped that day) and re-read **by file** against this run's thesis. Its deliverability corner is genuinely well-built — per-provider seed-list placement, Gmail Postmaster + Microsoft SNDS reputation trends, send-over-send regression deltas, every metric labeled Measured / User-provided / Estimated with `NEEDS_INPUT` rather than pass-by-default. And it contains **no MPP, machine-open, bot-click, or prefetch content at all**, including in its `send-experiment-designer`, which designs email experiments on an instrument it never questions. The market's best-resourced email collection does not carry the discipline; the two small lifecycle-native repos above do. |

## 2026-08-14 — focus: content / SEO-AEO-GEO (9 sources → 1 enhance (+1 co-credit), 1 watch, 6 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: content/SEO-AEO-GEO was last the *focus* on 2026-08-10 (second run), the oldest of the four (paid/social 08-11, PMM/sales/GTM 08-12, email/analytics/ops 08-13). Its queue entered this run holding the `rampstackco/claude-skills` read-properly item and the blocked `seo-programmatic-strategist` PROPOSAL.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-14 | [inhouseseo/superseo-skills](https://github.com/inhouseseo/superseo-skills) (254★, 11 skills, pushed 2026-05-12) — `eeat-audit` | Apache-2.0 (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Content quality / SEO | **enhanced-ours** | The find of the run. Its `eeat-audit` skill treats Experience/Expertise/Authoritativeness/Trust as **four separately-diagnosed dimensions with distinct evidence requirements and distinct fixes**, rather than as one "content quality" judgement — and singles out **Experience** as the underrated dimension, the one requiring evidence the author *performed* the work rather than researched it, with a supporting `experience-detection-playbook.md`. Gap grep-verified before writing and it was sharper than expected: **`E-E-A-T` appeared in exactly four places repo-wide and every one is a passing mention** (two one-line bullets in `seo-ai-search-optimizer` + the AEO playbook asserting "trust is the top E-E-A-T factor"; one inside a Google core-update quotation in `seo-technical-auditor`), while `EEAT`, `information gain`, `originality`, `first-hand`, `author bio`, and `semantic gap` returned **zero**. The load-bearing consequence: `seo-technical-auditor`'s sitewide-drop section (shipped 2026-08-11) routes an algorithm-update class to "the slow content and **E-E-A-T self-assessment** that `seo-content-optimizer` and `content-blog-strategist` own" — **a handoff pointing at two files that never used the vocabulary**, the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* pattern in its dead-ending-handoff form. Shipped **Critical Rule 10** + a **"Credibility Has Four Parts, and Only Two of Them Are the Page's"** section + a **Page Credibility Assessment** deliverable + a **Credibility routing** metric, in both dual-located copies of `seo-content-optimizer` (owner decided by the repo's own established seam: the Blog Strategist owns prospective prevention per its 2026-08-10 quality-floor section, this agent owns assessment of pages that already exist). **Six things are ours.** (a) **There is no score to raise** — Google documents that E-E-A-T "isn't a specific ranking factor" and that raters "have no control over how pages rank," so the output is a routed work list and never a grade; **both surveyed sources aggregate to a single number (a 40-point sum here, a weighted 0–100 in the co-credit below) and that is deliberately not adopted**. (b) **Trust is a gate, not an addend** — Google's "Of these aspects, trust is most important" is implemented as a *stop*, because any weighted total (even one weighting trust heaviest) lets strong Expertise arithmetically outvote a failed Trust check; this is explicitly the same defect the repo found and fixed in lead scoring on 2026-07-30, where fit and engagement summed into one number let engagement compensate for fit. (c) The **four-way owner routing table**, whose fourth row is the point: authoritativeness is *not fixable on the page*, so "improve E-E-A-T" landing whole on a writer is an unactionable ticket. (d) The **ghostwriting seam named rather than avoided** — this repo ships a `content-thought-leadership-ghostwriter`, so the line drawn is *the voice may be ghostwritten; the experience must be the byline's own*, with that agent's existing Rule 2 (60–90 min interview extracting "specific experience or case study informing perspective") identified as an Experience-harvesting instrument already in the repo and extended past executives to the practitioner who actually ran the thing. (e) The **B2B YMYL classes** — most of a SaaS library is not YMYL but security practice, compliance posture (GDPR/HIPAA/SOC 2), payments, payroll/benefits/hiring, healthcare data, and legal obligation are, and a uniformly-policed blog always under-polices exactly those. (f) **Unknown never rounds to pass** (the repo's standing discipline) and **no recovery date attached to credibility work**, which keeps faith with the technical auditor's own "there is no switch to flip" on algorithmic declines. Ideas-only, written from scratch; Apache-2.0 notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation and none was made. All quoted wording cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-14 ([Creating helpful, reliable, people-first content](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/fundamentals/creating-helpful-content)). No ranking, scoring, or recovery figure asserted. Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical. Noted for future runs: its `semantic-gap-analysis` and `expert-interview` skills, and a `content-brief` whose references are unusually deep. |
| 2026-08-14 | [AgricIDaniel/claude-seo](https://github.com/AgricIDaniel/claude-seo) (14.1k★, pushed 2026-07-27) — `skills/seo/references/eeat-framework.md` | MIT | Content quality / SEO | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | Read alongside the above as an **independent arrival at the same decomposition**, which is why the gap was treated as real rather than as one author's taxonomy: it too scores the four dimensions separately, too elevates Trust ("the most important member of the family," with E/E/A framed as supporting evidence for it), and too names Experience as the dimension that cannot be fabricated. Its enumeration of YMYL categories informed our own B2B-specific list. **Its aggregation was not adopted either** — a 30%-weighted Trust inside a 0–100 composite is the compensation defect the trust-gate exists to remove, and a 0–100 "Exceptional / Critical" band is precisely the score Google's own documentation says does not exist. A source already mined repeatedly (crawler access 07-30, cannibalization 08-02 via its sibling `claude-blog`); this is its first content-quality contribution. |

## 2026-08-16 — focus: paid media / social (11 sources → 1 enhance (+2 co-credit), 1 watch, 7 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: paid/social was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-11, the oldest of the four — PMM/sales/GTM ran 08-12, email/analytics/ops 08-13, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-14. Its queue held two items and both were **resolved rather than worked**: the `aaron-he-zhu` **learning-phase** watch grep-verified as **already closed** (the 08-11 ship to `paid-media-ppc-strategist` gave the state an owner, a "when the number isn't real yet" section, and cited platform durations — retired), and the `portermetricsample` **licence re-check** re-verified via the GitHub license API as still `null` (still ideas-only, still unread, item stays open).

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-16 | [gooseworks-ai/goose-skills](https://github.com/gooseworks-ai/goose-skills) (1,153★, pushed 2026-08-14) — `skills/ads/composites/meta-ad-policy-checker` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Paid / ad policy | **enhanced-ours** | First sighting, and the sharpest statement in the market that **pre-flight policy review is a discipline, not a checkbox**. Its method: three policy tiers (baseline applied to every ad / content-driven triggered by claims and keywords / category-driven activated by a declared Special Ad Category), a three-state severity model (block / fix-required / caution), **evidence-first findings** where every finding carries a direct policy quote and paraphrased findings are dropped, **intent-preserving rewrites** that change expression but never the offer or the audience, live policy fetch at runtime rather than hardcoded rules (because policy text drifts), and a structured verdict shaped for a downstream gate. Gap grep-verified before writing and it was stark: **`ad policy`, `account suspen`, and `restricted categor` returned zero hits repo-wide**; `disapprov` resolved to exactly three real places and every one is a passing mention — a "what doesn't work" bullet in `design-ad-creative-producer`, the **"Delivery halted"** driver row in the Budget Optimizer's pacing table shipped 08-11 ("an ops incident in a pacing costume… cheapest to rule out, so rule it out first"), and one launch-checklist row in `phase-4-launch` ("Check disapproval reason; revise and resubmit"); `policy violation` was that same row, and `appeal` resolved only to subreddit bans in `social-reddit-specialist`. The repo named the failure as a *driver to rule out* and a *one-line remediation* and nothing owned the prevention — the recurring **control named, instrument absent** pattern. The adjacent coverage that does exist is a **different rulebook**: `ops-legal-compliance` and `ops-quality-assurance` review material against law (FTC, GDPR, CAN-SPAM, trademark), which clears nothing on a platform. So `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` — the Meta/LinkedIn owner, unenhanced since 2026-07-26 while the PPC Strategist and Budget Optimizer were both worked 08-11 — got **Critical Rule 10** + an **"Approval Is Not Permission: Running Inside the Platform's Rulebook"** section + an **Ad Policy Pre-Flight & Enforcement Register** deliverable + two success metrics, in both dual-located copies. **Nine layers are ours.** (a) The organizing seam — **legal review is not a platform clearance**: two rulebooks, two consequence classes, and the platform's remedy is not a fine but switching the account off. (b) **Approved never rounds to compliant** — the repo's standing "unknown never rounds to pass" applied to a review verdict, because both platforms document re-review and removal at any time, making a live ad a *not-yet-rejected* ad and the policy read a thing maintained for the campaign's life rather than retired at launch. (c) The **blast-radius ordering rule** — account-scale checks before ad-scale ones, since a copy fix is cheap at any point and an account restriction is not recoverable on a campaign timeline. (d) The **B2B cost of an outage** — a two-week restriction inside a two-quarter sales cycle is not a spend gap that closes when the account returns, it is a pipeline gap that lands two quarters later in a period whose spend looked fine, and the Budget Optimizer's pacing row detects it only once the account is already dark. (e) **Your product's market is not your ad's category** — an ATS vendor selling hiring software is not in Employment until the same account runs its own recruiting ads, and a finance-automation platform is not offering a financial product until it promotes an embedded lending product; the declaration is *per campaign*, and the country scope means one creative can be in-category on a US-targeted campaign and out of it elsewhere. (f) **In-category, the B2B playbook mostly stops working** — the approved targeting set removes Lookalikes, location exclusion and radius precision, behaviour/demographic targeting and interest exclusion, which are the exact levers Rules 3, 4 and 8 build a precision B2B audience from, which makes the category a **media-plan decision, not a compliance checkbox**. (g) **The Proof Point Library is the substantiation record** LinkedIn's factual-support rule is asking for, so an ad claim with no row in it is a messaging failure and a policy exposure at once — with analyst and review-site badges as the perishable case (licensed, time-bound, tied to a report period). (h) **Vagueness is not a compliance strategy** — the B2B inversion of the consumer failure mode: softening a claim to clear review converts a policy problem into a Critical Rule 1 performance loss, so substantiate or remove. (i) **A competitor-name ad is a domain-scoped risk**, because an upheld Google trademark complaint restricts every ad sharing the second-level domain in its final URL, making it an account-level decision even when it lives in one campaign. The fourth disposition (**unreviewable**) is our own four-state control model (adopted 07-26) applied to a policy verdict, so an ambiguous clause, a gated landing page, or a legal-not-platform question keeps its own name. Ideas-only, written from scratch; the repo's `scripts/` and MCP-wired execution shape does not transfer. Every platform claim quoted from and cited to a primary source read 2026-08-16: Meta [Advertising Standards](https://transparency.meta.com/policies/ad-standards/) ("remain subject to review and re-review at all times"; a restricted asset "can't be used to advertise across our technologies"; Account Quality review), [Discriminatory Practices](https://transparency.meta.com/policies/ad-standards/unacceptable-content/discriminatory-practices/) (the self-identification requirement and its US/Canada/parts-of-Europe scope), [Special Ad Category](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/marketing-api/special-ad-category/) (per-campaign declaration; the approved targeting set), [LinkedIn Advertising Policies](https://www.linkedin.com/legal/ads-policy) (sole-discretion removal; suspension for repeat violations; "any claims in your ad must have factual support"; the competitive-claims rule), [Google Trademarks policy](https://support.google.com/adspolicy/answer/6118) (second-level-domain scope). **No approval, rejection, appeal-success, or restriction-frequency figure asserted** — neither platform publishes one, and the register is explicitly instructed not to promise a stakeholder an appeal rate. **Critical Rule 4 fixed in passing**: its "default targeting wastes 40-50% of budget" contradicted the file's *own* 08-03 footnote disclaiming exactly that class of figure; rewritten to point at the audit that measures it. Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; all eight new external URLs HTTP 200. |

## 2026-08-17 — focus: PMM / sales / GTM (12 sources → 1 **adopted-new**, 2 watch, 8 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM/sales/GTM was last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-12, the oldest of the four — email/analytics/ops ran 08-13, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-14, paid/social 08-16. **This run was an ADD by policy**: the previous four scout runs (08-13, 08-14 ×2, 08-16) were all enhancements, and the roster is meant to grow, not only get edited. The thesis was set by an internal finding rather than by a trending repo — the repo ships a **Monthly ABM loop** and a worked ABM campaign workflow, and `ops-legal-compliance` names "the ABM program" as a channel that must query the suppression record, yet **no agent owned the program**. Four independent permissively-licensed sources shipping ABM as a first-class role confirmed the gap was the market's view too, not one author's taxonomy.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-17 | [gtmagents/gtm-agents](https://github.com/gtmagents/gtm-agents) (374★, pushed 2026-04-03) — `plugins/abm-orchestration` (`agents/abm-strategist`, `skills/account-tiering`, `skills/signal-intel`) | Apache-2.0 (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | GTM / ABM | **adopted-new** | Re-opened a source last assessed 2026-08-12 as a `watch (stale)` for its advocacy corner; read on this run's thesis instead and it is the most *structurally* complete ABM treatment surveyed — a dedicated plugin with a strategist, an analyst and an account planner over tiering, personalization and signal-intelligence skills. Three ideas were worth learning and are ours in expression only: **capacity planning as a tiering input** ("match tiers to owner bandwidth"), **SLA definitions per tier** (touch frequency, personalization level, reporting cadence), and the signal discipline of **combining at least two signal types before triggering a high-effort play** with per-source decay. Its outputs stop at a tiered plan and a RACI; ours adds the executability check, the credit rule and the retirement review. No text reused. |
| 2026-08-17 | [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills) (38★, pushed 2026-07-20) — `skills/abm/abm-1-to-1`, `abm-1-to-few`, `abm-1-to-many` | MIT (re-verified via the GitHub license API this run) | GTM / ABM | **adopted-new** (co-credit) | Third re-opening of this source (sales corner mined 07-28, growth corner 08-12) and the first read of its `abm/` tree. Ships the three motions as three separate skills, each with its own depth expectation — the clearest statement in the market that **tier is a resource decision, not a label**. Also the source of the pitfalls our Rules 3, 4 and 5 answer directly: ABM run as a marketing-only initiative, one-size-fits-all tiering, and measuring ABM on MQLs. Its Tier 1 phases (account deep dive → account plan → executive engagement → custom content → multi-thread orchestration) informed our Tier 1 Account Plan deliverable. Its gifting/Sendoso material was **deliberately not adopted** — vendor-bound, and gift policy is `ops-legal-compliance` ground. |
| 2026-08-17 | [lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth](https://github.com/lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth) (1★, pushed 2026-08-15) — `skills/abm-strategist` (+ `prompts/tiering`, `prompts/attribution`, `prompts/orchestration`, `prompts/signals-matrix`) | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | ABM | **adopted-new** (co-credit) | Second sighting of the source that supplied the advocacy thesis on 2026-08-12, and again the sharpest single sentence in the survey: **"Attribution that double-counts paid is worse than no attribution."** Its attribution prompt — inventory every channel claiming credit for these accounts, one primary source per opportunity, assists get assist credit and not pipeline dollars, pre/post lift on the named list as the preferred ABM method — is the direct ancestor of our Rule 9, which adds the matched-holdout option and routes the model itself to `paid-media-attribution-analyst`. Its "don't re-tier what the list owner tiered" seam validated our decision to give the list a single owner rather than spread it across consumers. |
| 2026-08-17 | [modest-curator478/claude-skills](https://github.com/modest-curator478/claude-skills) (3★, pushed 2026-08-15) — `revops/abm-campaign-builder` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | RevOps / ABM | **adopted-new** (co-credit) — *closes the 08-13 `watch`* | The `revops/` half of the watch filed 2026-08-13 is now read, and it is the strongest **prose** statement of the thesis: account selection is "where most ABM programs fail," lists get built from "a spreadsheet of logos someone wants to land," and "an ABM list is only as good as the coverage behind it — if sales can genuinely work 50 accounts at a time, the list should be 50 accounts, not 500." Also the source of the measurement framing our Rule 4 hardens (coverage, penetration, pipeline from target accounts, win rate and cycle length; explicitly *not* MQLs) and of the tier-specific coordination cadence. Its `leadership/` tree is **not** closed by this run — it remains the second independent arrival at the open "grade the marketing function" SCOPE QUESTION. |
| 2026-08-17 | [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) (44.5k★, pushed 2026-07-29) — `skills/ads/references/abm-playbook.md` | MIT | Paid / ABM | **dismissed** (wrong owner — routed, not adopted) | Sixth assessment of the category's front door, and the first time it had something on-thesis. Read in full: it is a genuinely detailed *paid* ABM playbook — LinkedIn list mechanics and match rates, Meta identity-graph enrichment, acceleration campaigns against open pipeline, frequency-capping via a self-rotating exclusion audience, channel-split guidance. Every one of those is `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` ground, and adopting them into a program-level agent would have broken the boundary rule the new agent is built around. **One fact was worth carrying across the seam** and was verified independently against the primary source rather than taken from here: the LinkedIn matched-audience floor, which is why a Tier 1 list is not a servable paid audience. Its ABM go/no-go thresholds (list size, deal size, budget) were **not** adopted — unsourced round numbers. Filed as a future enhancement lead for the paid agent. |
| 2026-08-17 | [ivangfalco/curated-abm-skills](https://github.com/ivangfalco/curated-abm-skills) (1★, pushed 2026-07-28), [vynx-hq/abm-skill](https://github.com/vynx-hq/abm-skill) (0★, pushed 2026-04-16) | **none / unlicensed** (both: license API returns null) | ABM | **dismissed** (unlicensed — unread) | The only two repos on GitHub whose *subject* is ABM. Both assert no licence, so by our rules they are ideas-only with no content reuse and both were **left unread** — recorded honestly. `vynx-hq` has not been pushed in four months; `ivangfalco` describes itself as a curation of LinkedIn/Meta ABM ad kits, i.e. the paid-channel corner already owned here. Re-check licence on a future PMM/GTM run only if either becomes active. |
| 2026-08-17 | [OneWave-AI/claude-skills](https://github.com/OneWave-AI/claude-skills) (255★, pushed 2026-08-11) | MIT | Sales / marketing (multi) | **dismissed** (no coverage) — *corroborates the gap* | Re-checked on this run's thesis after being dismissed 08-12 as thin on advocacy. Its entire account surface is one `expansion-revenue-finder/references/account-profile.md` — an installed-base expansion artifact, which is `growth-customer-marketing-lead` ground, not ABM. A 200+-skill collection with no target-account-list discipline is a useful negative data point: breadth-first collections skip the program layer and ship only the channel layer. |

Architecturally it is the executable direction this repo has repeatedly declined (Playwright journey discovery, XLSX round-trip delivery, plan-diff and drift scripts, canonical JSON schemas) — so nothing structural was adopted. What *was* adopted, ideas-only: **start from business journeys and analysis decisions, not click inventories**; **official-first**, with a custom event or parameter introduced only after documenting which official construct could not carry the meaning; **finite value domains** captured with an explicit complete/incomplete/over-N distinction so "sampled or disagreeing instances cannot be described as exhaustive" (our *unknown never rounds to pass* arrived at independently, applied to allowed-value enumeration); and **drift reported without silently mutating the plan**. Its explicit boundaries (does not configure or audit GTM, makes no legal or privacy approval decisions, does not maximise event counts) corroborate boundaries this file already held. The three-shapes decomposition was adopted ideas-only; its severity convention (WARNING reserved for something actually being wrong, never for a merely-absent optional parameter) and its "(tier inferred from notes — verify)" flag are the repo's own four-state / unknown-never-rounds-to-pass discipline arrived at independently, and were noted rather than adopted. Script-heavy and post-hoc by design; no content reused. |

| 2026-08-19 | [OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills) (650★, pushed 2026-08-17) — `skills/podcast-marketing` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Organic social / audio | **adopted-new** (primary) | The fullest podcast channel treatment surveyed: episode architecture for interview vs. solo, production thresholds, a guest-acquisition workflow, show-notes SEO structure, a ranked growth playbook, and a monetization ladder. Read in full and **deliberately inverted rather than adapted** — its centre of gravity is a *creator media business* (sponsorship rates at download tiers, host-read CPMs, "downloads in the first 7 days determine chart ranking and sponsorship pricing"), which is the exact audience mismatch this ledger keeps recording, and for B2B SaaS it is not merely irrelevant but actively wrong: a show that optimizes for chart position will book guests for reach instead of for relationship, which is the single decision that determines whether a B2B show returns anything. That inversion is the origin of **Critical Rule 4** (a download is a server-log file request, not a person) and **Rule 2** (the guest list is the strategy; reach is not a booking criterion). Its genuinely portable ideas — banking episodes before launch, transcripts as long-tail surface, video-by-default — were re-derived from scratch into our schema. Ideas only, no text reused. |
| 2026-08-19 | [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (29★, 106 skills, pushed 2026-08-13) — `skills/podcast-and-audiograms` + its `references/scope-and-connections.md` | MIT (re-verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Organic social / audio | **adopted-new** (co-credit) — *closes the 2026-08-07 `watch`* | **Re-opened the standing backlog item** ("read the B2B-relevant slice on a future social run", surfaced 2026-08-07, left open through three social-adjacent runs). Its podcast corner is the one slice that survived the creator/DTC audience filter that dismissed the rest of the repo. Two things worth learning, both structural rather than substantive: **retention-led clip selection** (choose moments from listener-retention data, not from an AI virality score) and — more useful to us — an unusually disciplined **scope-and-connections reference** that explicitly disclaims recording, transcription, hosting and full-episode strategy and routes each to a named sibling. That seam-drawing habit is the shape of our **Critical Rule 6** (record video by default; the YouTube channel belongs to `social-youtube-producer`) and **Rule 10** (nine named non-overlap boundaries). The skill is downstream-only by design: it starts after the show exists, which is precisely the half nobody in the market owns. Ideas only. |

Transcript → seven repurposed assets (blog, social, newsletter, show notes, audiograms, SEO elements), framed as an episode-as-repurposing-hub where the formats reinforce each other rather than standing alone. That framing is behind our **Episode Asset Pack** deliverable. Purely production-layer: no strategy, no measurement, no guest sourcing, nothing about whether the show should exist. Ideas only. |

## 2026-08-21 — focus: email / analytics / marketing-ops (12 sources → 1 enhance (+3 co-credit), 2 watch, 8 dismissed)

Discipline chosen by rotation: email/analytics/ops was last the *focus* on 2026-08-13, the oldest of the four — content/SEO ran 08-14 and 08-18, paid/social 08-16, PMM/sales/GTM 08-17, organic social 08-19. **This run was an ENHANCE by the alternation rule** (08-18 enhanced, 08-19 added). Its queue entered the run holding one actionable item — the standing 2026-08-18 `watch` on the `sidchaudhary/gtm-skills` reference tree, explicitly filed *for a future analytics/ops run* — and that item set the thesis. Two of its three named files were read in full this run; the third was read and re-filed for a different discipline.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|------|--------|---------|------------|---------|-------|
| 2026-08-21 | [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) (0★, pushed 2026-08-19) — `references/bayesian-testing.md` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Analytics / experimentation | **enhanced-ours** (primary) | The deepest Bayesian experimentation reference surveyed: Beta-posterior state, P(best) by Monte Carlo, **expected loss**, per-channel guardrail thresholds, holdout mutual exclusivity, exit rules in priority order, and a validity-threats section. Three ideas were genuinely new to us and all three shipped: **expected loss** as the decision-relevant quantity (what being wrong costs, in the metric's own units) rather than a second significance test; **practical equivalence** as a *terminal* exit state — every arm's expected loss below a declared tolerance means keep the incumbent and retire the question, which refines our existing `stop` verdict; and the sharp observation that **adaptive allocation does not remove the SRM check, it changes its reference** — compare observed exposures against what the allocator *intended* per period, and where the platform cannot report intended allocation, say SRM is unverifiable rather than skipping it. Its own framing is a high-volume lifecycle/e-commerce vendor doc (revenue-per-send goal hierarchy, Thompson sampling as a default, 500-per-variant floors), and its "use Bayesian when traffic is low" line is the claim we inverted — see the *A posterior is not more data* subsection. **Closes the Bayesian slice of the 2026-08-18 watch.** Ideas only, no text reused. |
| 2026-08-21 | [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) (45,102★, pushed 2026-08-19) — `skills/ab-testing/references/sample-size-guide.md` | MIT | Analytics / experimentation | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | The most-starred marketing skill collection in the market, re-read on its sample-size corner. It is the only source that states the honest option plainly: *"Don't test: make decision based on qualitative data instead"* sits at #6 of a seven-option list for when the requirement exceeds the traffic. What it does not do is **gate** — the seven options are a flat menu with no trigger, no ranking, and no distinction between the moves that preserve an honest read and the ones that only look like they do; *"Lower confidence: use 90% instead of 95%"* is listed at #2 as a peer of #6. Our version keeps the menu, derives a trigger for it, ranks the reroutes by the inverse-square property, and refuses the loosen-α move outright. Its quick-reference tables disagree with the standard formula's output (their 3% / 20% row reads ~31,000 per arm against ~13,900 recomputed), which is a second reason we recompute rather than import any table. Ideas only. |
| 2026-08-21 | [OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills) (658★, pushed 2026-08-17) — `skills/ab-test-setup` | MIT | Analytics / experimentation | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | The only source in the survey that puts a **ceiling** on test duration and makes exceeding it a decision point: *"If duration exceeds 8 weeks: increase MDE, reduce variants, test a higher-traffic page, use a micro-conversion metric, or accept lower power."* That is the right shape and the wrong ceiling — eight weeks is a fixed number with no derivation, and "accept lower power" is again offered as a peer option. The idea we took is the ceiling itself; what we contributed is deriving it from the surface's **comparability horizon** (the longest window it has historically gone unchanged), which makes it a property of the page rather than a convention, and connects it to `analytics-performance-analyst`'s series-break register. Ideas only. |
| 2026-08-21 | [thatrebeccarae/claude-marketing](https://github.com/thatrebeccarae/claude-marketing) (114★, pushed 2026-05-14) — `skills/ab-testing-framework` | MIT | Analytics / experimentation | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit, as the claim inverted) | Previously mined for its read-only connector posture (2026-07-25). Re-read here for its explicit frequentist/Bayesian split, which states the market's standard advice in its clearest form: **"When to use Bayesian: low traffic (small sample sizes)."** Independent arrival at the same claim as the gtm-skills reference, which is what made it worth answering rather than ignoring. The answer shipped as *A posterior is not more data*: Bayes changes the shape of the output, not the amount of evidence, and at small N the prior does a material share of the work — so declare the prior before the data and never report P(best) without expected loss beside it. Repo itself has not moved since May 2026; no other new substance. Ideas only. |
| 2026-08-21 | [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) — `references/data-input-integrity.md` | MIT | Analytics / ops | **dismissed** (substantially covered) → **watch** on a residual | Read in full, as the 2026-08-18 item asked. Seven pre-analysis checks on a pasted export. Five are already ours in stronger form: the partial-period trap and the not-yet-matured cohort are `analytics-performance-analyst`'s **maturity** section; timezone alignment across sources, identity and dedup rules are its **comparability freeze**; *always show the denominator* and *rank by absolute impact, not rate* are its **absolute-floor** rule; *date the row to the sale, not to the consequence* is the same agent's flow-boundary discipline; and the closing *state a blocker rather than a caveat under a confident number* is our standing **unknown never rounds to pass**. The residual is e-commerce-shaped (currency mixing, tax/shipping inclusion, line-item-vs-order granularity). Two residual checks are plausibly ours and were filed as a P2 watch rather than crammed into this run: **silent export truncation by a row limit**, and **placeholder values parsed as numbers** (`0`, `9999`, `N/A`) in a field a calculation depends on. **Watch closed 2026-08-21** — both shipped as a "Read the export before you read the series" subsection in both copies of `analytics-performance-analyst`, placed upstream of the comparability freeze; the e-commerce residual (currency / tax-shipping / order-vs-line-item) stays not-taken. This source tree is now fully worked (the Bayesian slice closed earlier the same day). |
| 2026-08-21 | [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) — `references/audit-findings-discipline.md` | MIT | Ops / QA | **watch** (different discipline) | Read, and it is good: an audit carries an **expiry expressed as a re-audit trigger** (the event that makes it stale — a checkout change, a policy update — not a fixed interval), a **scope statement specific enough to reproduce** ("mobile Safari, guest checkout, one product, UK address", not "the checkout"), and findings ranked on **severity × effort** in a four-cell grid (do now / plan / cheap wins / leave) rather than a flat severity list nobody finishes. On-thesis for `ops-quality-assurance` and `seo-technical-auditor`, both of which produce audits with no stated expiry. Held for an **ops** run — one change per run, and this is not this run's discipline. |
| 2026-08-21 | [ericosiu/ai-marketing-skills](https://github.com/ericosiu/ai-marketing-skills) (3,413★, pushed 2026-08-16) — `growth-engine/experiment-engine.py` | MIT | Growth / experimentation | **dismissed** (illustrates the failure mode) | Previously dismissed twice as breadth-without-depth (07-30, 08-05); re-read this run on its Experiment Engine specifically, which is the collection's on-thesis component. It is a real scoring harness — bootstrap confidence intervals on lift, an explicit per-variant sample floor, a separate `TRENDING` state below the floor, env-configurable thresholds — and its defaults are `min_samples` of 10–15 per variant with auto-promotion of a winner into a "living playbook" at p < 0.05. Fair to the author: the floor is explicit and configurable, which is more than most. But a winner auto-promoted off ten observations per arm is precisely the artifact our new Rule 10 exists to prevent, and a harness that *promotes* it into a reusable playbook propagates the error. Recorded as corroborating evidence that the gap is real in shipped tooling, not as a source to adapt. |

Unchanged verdict. |

Its channel-selection section names **five** B2B paid channel families, two of which this repo had no owner for: **paid review listings (G2, Capterra, Software Advice — "often skipped", high intent)** and **sponsorships (newsletters, podcasts, events, creators)**. That is the clearest statement in the market that this cluster is a *channel*, not a miscellany, and it is why the new agent exists. Its own ads content is auction-platform ground we already hold; the `~$100 channel test` heuristic was **not** adopted (an unsourced round number, and a single small test is exactly what the new agent's Rule 6 refuses). |

Its cited CPL / lead-to-SQO / consumption-gap figures come from vendor reports (NetLine, LeadSpot) and were **deliberately not carried** — no benchmark figure appears in our file. |

Four portable mechanics behind Rules 1 and 2: **check the pulse before the price** (an outlet's landing page outlives its publishing), the **provenance ladder** for quoted numbers (public rate card > directory > the seller's claim), the **package-versus-single-placement** price trap, and median-over-mean where one outlier distorts. ## 2026-08-24 — PMM / sales / GTM run (**ENHANCE**: `pmm-competitive-intelligence` — the win/loss program's missing instrument)

Focus discipline **PMM / sales / GTM** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-17, the oldest of the four (content/SEO 08-18, organic social 08-19, email/analytics/ops 08-21, paid media/social 08-23). The 08-23 scout run **ADDED**, so the alternation rule put an **ENHANCE** first this run. **25 sources evaluated across 13 rows.** Thesis set by a grep, not by a repo: `win/loss` appears in **eight** of our canonical agents and in the `product-marketing-ops` and `sales-enablement` skill routers, and not one of them says who conducts the interview, whom you sample, or what the sample can support.

| 2026-08-24 | [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) (0★, pushed 2026-08-21) — `skills/account-executive/win-loss-analysis` + `references/customer-research-methods.md` | MIT (verified via the GitHub license API this run) | Sales / PMM | **enhanced-ours** (primary) | Fourth productive re-opening of this source (analytics 08-21 ×2, ops watch 08-21). By far the most complete statement in the market of the evidence-provenance problem, and five of its moves are credited: a recorded close reason is **a claim, not a fact** (filled in by the person who lost the deal, while closing it out); **"price" is the default, not usually the reason**, with an explicit *price, unevidenced* bucket and the finding that if that bucket leads, loss reasons are not being captured; **the rep must not conduct the interview**; **no-decision reported as its own row**, never sharing with price or a competitor; **say what the sample cannot see** (closed deals only — excludes open deals that will die and buyers who never entered pipeline); plus stated-versus-revealed preference, ranking by value alongside count, and splitting the window at a material pricing/positioning change. What it does **not** do is separate the three evidence records by the question each can answer, or notice that its own skill computes a win rate from the same small sample it ranks reasons in. Its three headline accuracy percentages (~42% / ~65% / ~15%) are **unsourced pack benchmarks — the file itself says so — and were deliberately not carried**; nor was its vendor attribution block. Ideas only, no text reused. |
| 2026-08-24 | [pmalliance/product-marketing-skills](https://github.com/pmalliance/product-marketing-skills) (62★) — `pmm-signal-pack/skills/win-loss-reasons` | MIT | PMM | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | Re-opened after being dismissed as *have* on 2026-07-31 — correctly at the time, since its core move (separate **what buyers said** from **what the pattern shows**) is genuinely covered by our Rule 5 and the JOLT no-decision work. Re-read here for method rather than thesis, and the portable part is its evidence standard: every reason in the table cites a specific deal, call date, or transcript line, and anything untraceable goes to a short *worth checking* list rather than into the ranking. Behind our provenance-labelling requirement. Its whole evidence base is still internal artifacts — transcripts, deal notes, CRM close reasons — with no buyer interview anywhere in the process, which is the gap our section is about. |
| 2026-08-24 | [matteotitta/genesys-skills](https://github.com/matteotitta/genesys-skills) (31★, 168 skills, pushed 2026-07-31) — `skills/research/win-loss` | MIT | PMM / GTM | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | Re-opened (first mined 2026-08-04). A transcript-analysis engine: six analysis dimensions, frequency-with-confidence reporting (3+ calls = high, 2 = emerging, 1 = "single mention — pattern unconfirmed"), a 5-wins-and-5-losses floor, and a **correlation-as-causation** guard ("use *associated with*, not *caused by*"). Two credited ideas: state frequency on every finding, and never let a single mention read as a pattern. Its own instruction to **cross-tabulate across at least three dimensions** is the move our analysis-unit arithmetic refuses at B2B interview volumes — cross-tabulating 20 interviews three ways empties most cells — so it is credited as the claim answered rather than adopted. Its evidence base is again transcripts only. |
