| 2026-08-24 | [ICC/ESOMAR International Code on Market, Opinion and Social Research and Data Analytics](https://community.esomar.org/uploads/public/knowledge-and-standards/codes-and-guidelines/ICCESOMAR_Code_English_.pdf) (© ICC/ESOMAR 2016) | Professional standard (primary source, not a code repo) | Research ethics | **adopted** (primary citation) | Downloaded and read in full this run. Supplies the grounding no surveyed marketing skill has: the rep-must-not-interview rule is not only data hygiene, it is the research/selling separation the profession's own code requires. Quoted verbatim: Art. 1(c) researchers "must remain diligent in maintaining the distinction between research and non-research activities"; Art. 1(d) where they do both they must "clearly distinguish and separate those activities from research"; Art. 4(a) identify yourself promptly, "clearly state the general purpose of the research as soon as methodologically possible," and "respect the right of data subjects to refuse requests to participate in research"; Art. 6(a) disclose and obtain consent for any non-research use *before* collection; and the footnote quoting ICC marketing-code Art. 9 that "a communication promoting the sale of a product should not be disguised as for example market research, consumer surveys…". Cited as a professional standard binding its signatories, **explicitly not as law**. A **fifth edition revised 2025-09-29** exists (confirmed on ICC's site this run) but its download is gated behind a language-selection form, so it was **not read** — flagged in-file for re-verification at next review. |

## 2026-08-26 — email / analytics / marketing-ops run (**ADD**: `analytics-martech-stack-strategist`)

Focus discipline **email / analytics / marketing-ops** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-21, the oldest of the four (paid media/social 08-23, PMM/sales/GTM 08-24, content/SEO 08-25). The 08-24 and 08-25 scout runs were **both enhancements**, so the alternation rule put an **ADD** first this run. Thesis set by a grep, not by a repo: **`martech` returned zero hits repo-wide** outside `maintenance/`, as did `tool consolidation` and `database health` — the repo has an agent whose frontmatter claimed "marketing technology stack" and whose 8,600 words are entirely about the data model *inside* the stack. **13 sources evaluated across 9 rows.**

| 2026-08-26 | [NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills](https://github.com/NEON-Rutger/B2B-revops-skills) (45★, 38 skills, created 2026-04-02, pushed 2026-08-24) — `revops-tech-stack`, `crm-migration-consolidation` | MIT (verified via the GitHub licence API this run) | RevOps / martech | **adopted-new** (primary) | First sighting, and the most substantial statement in this survey of the stack as an owned portfolio. Four credited ideas, all rewritten from scratch: **capability-first evaluation** ("what capability do we need, how well does our current stack deliver it" rather than which tool to buy); **a tool without a defined process is shelfware**; **never let a satellite tool become the system of record**; and, from the migration skill, **consolidate-vs-coexist is a business decision that precedes "how"** plus harmonize-before-cutover (migrate-then-clean is the anti-pattern). Its per-object system-of-record table was **not** adopted — our `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` already carries "an authoritative system per fact, not per tool" from the 08-18 instrumentation-contract ship, and re-deriving it would gild. Its headline figures — "55% of CRM initiatives fail (Gartner, 2026)", "12 to 18 months (PMI Stack, 2026)", "post-migration data work costs 3x (industry data)", "exact-key matching catches only 60 to 70% (entity resolution research, 2026)", and its per-layer ARR/headcount adoption triggers — are vaguely attributed or unsourced and were **deliberately not carried**. |

Heavily Braze/Stripo/SFMC vendor-bound overall, but its two vendor-neutral skills are the best migration material surveyed anywhere. Credited: **shadow tools are where data leakage originates**, so the inventory includes what nobody approved; **what happens if the handoff fails** and **flag any integration with no documented retry logic** as first-class audit questions; redundancy framed as **two tools sending to the same audience bypass either one's frequency cap** rather than only as duplicate licence cost; and, from `esp-migration`, the **parallel/dual-send transfers reputation, not recipients** shape, **suppression-list sync as the fatal miss**, **don't migrate engagement history for targeting**, and **personalization dialects must be re-authored, not ported** (merge tags / Liquid / AMPscript / Django-derived, with an unresolved token rendering blank rather than erroring). Its Orbit tool calls, per-vendor API quirks and 50-record data-quality scoring convention are executable-layer and product-bound; not taken. |

Focus discipline **paid media / social** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-23, the oldest of the four (PMM/sales/GTM 08-24, content/SEO 08-25, email/analytics/ops 08-26). The 08-26 scout run **ADDED** (`analytics-martech-stack-strategist`), so the alternation rule put an **ENHANCE** first this run. 19 sources evaluated; the paid-media half of the discipline was found fully worked from prior runs (`mardab96`, `itallstartedwithaidea`, `hayesti54-eng`, `fourteenwm`, `TheMattBerman`, `AgriciDaniel` all previously closed), so the opportunity was on the **social / creator** half, which had never been surveyed for vetting method.

| 2026-08-27 | [ScrapeCreators/social-media-research-skills](https://github.com/ScrapeCreators/social-media-research-skills) (1,785★, 13 skills, created 2026-06-06, pushed 2026-08-26) — `outlier-post-finder`, `influencer-prospecting`, `creator-profile-teardown` | MIT (verified via the GitHub licence API this run) | Social / creator research | **enhanced-ours** (primary) | First sighting, and the highest-starred source ever adopted here. Three ideas taken, all ideas-only and rewritten from scratch: (a) **an outlier is defined against the account's own baseline, not by sorting on views** — the skill states its goal explicitly as *not* sorting by views, which is the argument behind our median-and-spread read; (b) prospect quality is **several independent dimensions** (niche fit, audience fit, reach, engagement quality, brand safety, contactability) rather than one reach number; (c) comment quality is a first-class signal, not a count. Everything else declined: the ScrapeCreators API layer, the per-platform endpoint tables, the 1-5 numeric scorecard (a rubric would re-create the false precision the change removes), and the consumer-creator framing throughout. Its `ad-library-teardown` is **have** — `pmm-competitive-intelligence` already names the LinkedIn and Meta Ad Libraries. Remaining nine skills left on **watch**. |
| 2026-08-27 | [rdsolod-ui/insta-parcel-skill](https://github.com/rdsolod-ui/insta-parcel-skill) (3★, created + pushed 2026-07-23) — `docs/discovery-and-quality.md`, `references/evidence-policy.md` | MIT (verified via the GitHub licence API this run) | Influencer vetting / research ethics | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | First sighting. Star count is not evidence and this repo has adopted from 1-2★ sources before on substance. Two ideas: a **source-strength hierarchy** (official export > approved API > the creator's own public post > a verified third-party mention > a search result, with "appeared in search / a hashtag / a recommendation" explicitly **not** evidence of anything), and a **disallowed-inference boundary** — do not label a person with a sensitive trait, do not use appearance or social connections as a proxy, exclude minors. That second idea supplied the half of our section that has no analogue in the market: vetting an outlet audits a business, vetting a creator compiles a file on a **named individual**, and the two are not governed the same way. Declined: the Instagram data contract, the CSV validation scripts and the Python tooling (executable direction, repeatedly declined here). |
| 2026-08-27 | [16 CFR § 465.8](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/465.8) and [§ 465.1](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/465.1) — FTC Rule on the Use of Consumer Reviews and Testimonials | Primary source (federal regulation) | Legal / social | **adopted** (primary citation) | Read this run for the fake-engagement rule. § 465.8(b) makes it an unfair or deceptive act to "purchase or procure fake indicators of social media influence that they knew or should have known to be fake and that materially misrepresent their influence or importance for a commercial purpose"; § 465.1 defines those indicators to cover followers, connections, subscribers, views, likes, shares and comments generated by bots or accounts that "otherwise do not reflect a real individual's or entity's activities". Part 465 was already cited in `pmm-customer-advocacy` (incentivised reviews, review suppression) — § 465.8 is new ground and belongs to the influencer agent. **Contested reach flagged, not asserted:** whether the provision reaches *paying a creator* whose indicators were inflated is stated as `ops-legal-compliance`'s verdict. |
| 2026-08-27 | [16 CFR § 255.1(d)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/16/255.1) — FTC Guides Concerning Use of Endorsements and Testimonials | Primary source (federal regulation) | Legal / social | **adopted** (primary citation) | Read this run for advertiser liability. Quoted verbatim: "Advertisers are subject to liability for misleading or unsubstantiated statements made through endorsements or for failing to disclose unexpected material connections between themselves and their endorsers," with the standing direction to provide guidance, **monitor endorsers' compliance**, and remedy non-compliance. The monitoring half is what turned disclosure from a contract clause into an operated control. § 255.5 (material connections) was left where `social-podcast-strategist` Rule 8 already states it, and cross-referenced rather than duplicated. |

Quoted: members must not "artificially increase engagement" and must not "agree with others ahead of time to like or re-share each other's content" — which is the pod arrangement described in LinkedIn's own words, on the platform where a B2B creator partnership is most likely to run. |

## 2026-08-28 — PMM / sales / GTM run (**ADD**: `sales-solutions-engineer`)

Focus discipline **PMM / sales / GTM** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-24, the oldest of the four (content/SEO 08-25, email/analytics/ops 08-26, paid media/social 08-27). The 08-27 scout run **ENHANCED** (`social-influencer-partnerships`) and the 08-26 run **ADDED**, so alternation put an **ADD** first this run. Thesis set by a grep: **`sales engineer` / `solutions engineer` / `solution consultant` returned zero hits repo-wide**, and so did **`proof of concept`**, while POCs, demos and security questionnaires appeared as checklist items inside four different agents — the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* pattern. 14 sources evaluated across 8 rows.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |

Read `part3-presenting-demos` and `part4-proving-value` in full. Supplied the program shapes: the pre-demo gate (do we know the pain, the room, what "good" means, and the agreed outcome), the demo as a route with a destination rather than a feature tour, the POC as a written project plan with signed success criteria, the pre-close question asked of the evaluation, and the technical win as an outcome distinct from the deal. It is itself a study aid for John Care and Aron Bohlig's *Mastering Technical Sales* — those frameworks belong to the authors and are credited as theirs in the shipped file, ideas only, no prose reused. |

| 2026-08-29 | [Regulation (EU) 2016/679 (GDPR)](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?uri=CELEX:32016R0679), Article 3(2)(a) + Recital 23 · [Directive 2002/58/EC (ePrivacy)](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?uri=CELEX:02002L0058-20091219) consolidated to 2009-12-19, Article 13(1), 13(2), 13(5) | EU primary law (not a code repo) | Legal grounding | **adopted** (primary citations) | Both read in full on EUR-Lex this run and quoted verbatim in the new agent. GDPR Recital 23 names *language, currency and the mentioning of customers in the Union* as evidence that a non-EU controller "envisages offering goods or services to data subjects in the Union" — which is what makes the localization act itself the compliance trigger, the sharpest available argument for sequencing the question before the campaign rather than after it. ePrivacy Art. 13(5) applies the Art. 13(1) prior-consent rule to *natural persons* and leaves "subscribers other than natural persons" to Member-State law, which is why B2B cold-email legality is a **per-country** question inside the EU. The **consolidated** ePrivacy text was deliberately used rather than the 2002 original, applying the lesson from the ICC/ESOMAR edition slip (backlog line 112). Cited as law that raises a question; the agent answers none of it and routes every determination to `ops-legal-compliance`. |

The load-bearing pair is *"Avoid automatically redirecting users from one language version of a site to a different language version of a site"* together with the crawling fact on the same page — *"Google crawls the web from different locations around the world. We do not attempt to vary the crawler source"* — which is **why** an IP-based redirect is fatal rather than merely rude: the crawler arrives from one place, is redirected on arrival, and never fetches the other locales. Also: *"Each language version must list itself as well as all other language versions"*, *"If two pages don't both point to each other, the tags will be ignored"*, the `x-default` definition, the fully-qualified-URL requirement, and the spam-policy example naming *"automated transformations like synonymizing, translating, or other obfuscation techniques... where little value is provided to users"*. |

## 2026-08-31 — email / analytics / marketing-ops run (**ENHANCE**: `analytics-customer-insights-researcher` — the rule meant to stop anecdotes was itself instructing a fabricated statistic)

Focus discipline **email / analytics / marketing-ops** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-26, the oldest of the four (paid/social 08-27, PMM/sales/GTM 08-28 and 08-29, content/SEO 08-30). The 08-30 scout run **ENHANCED**, so the alternation rule opened this run looking for an **ADD** — and, after four candidate roles were grep-tested and killed as already-owned (below), the honest verdict was that this discipline has no unowned role worth a persona right now, so the run shipped the highest-value enhancement instead. Thesis set by reading our own file rather than by a trending repo: `analytics-customer-insights-researcher` was the thinnest agent in the analytics discipline (1,333 words against 1,221–8,633 for its five siblings) and its **Rule 5 instructed a methodological error** — *"Report findings as: '80% of customers mentioned X'"* — i.e. the rule written to stop anecdotes told the reader to manufacture a prevalence statistic out of a purposive sample, in a repo whose entire recent history is anti-fabrication sweeps. Its Rule 3 named "representative sample" with no sampling instrument behind it, and the grep says the whole repo is thin here rather than only this agent: `sampling frame` returns **one** file (`pmm-pricing-packaging-strategist`), `non-response` **one** (`pmm-competitive-intelligence`, on win/loss), `self-selection` **one** (`social-linkedin-strategist`), and `saturation` **four** — all of them paid-media files using the word in the unrelated audience/creative sense. **None of the four appears in this agent at all**; it names *response rate* exactly once, as a line item inside a deliverable, with no method anywhere behind it. **14 sources evaluated across 8 rows.**

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-31 | [roy-tong/user-demand-research](https://github.com/roy-tong/user-demand-research) (0★, MIT verified via the GitHub licence API this run, pushed 2026-08-27) — `skills/user-demand-research/SKILL.md` (the SURE protocol) | MIT | Customer research / demand evidence | **enhanced-ours** (primary) | First sighting and the sharpest methodological source surveyed in this discipline. Read in full. Two things carried as ideas: an **evidence ladder** in which context, a stated problem, an observed substitute or workaround, a stated preference, and money/retained behaviour are different strengths and *a lower rung is never promoted to a higher one* — which is the instrument our new Rule 9 needed; and the flat prohibition on inferring **prevalence, market share, or percentages from convenience samples**, which is precisely the defect our Rule 5 was instructing. Also corroborated the point that many routes inside one platform are within-platform coverage rather than independent sources. Its E0–E5 notation, its `study.json` / `sure.py` stage-gated CLI, its platform connector registry, its Chinese-language report assembly and its scraping-policy layer are execution machinery and were **not** taken — this repo ships advisory personas, not runbooks over a data plane. No prose reused. |
| 2026-08-31 | [Aatirs-Vault/voc-claude-skills](https://github.com/Aatirs-Vault/voc-claude-skills) (0★, MIT, pushed 2026-08-18) — `voc-synthesize/SKILL.md` + `config/thresholds.json` | MIT | Voice of customer | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | First sighting. Three ideas carried, all of them instruments our Rule 5 lacked: **independence is counted in channels, not records** (two calls are one source; one call plus one review are two) — the single most useful correction available to a "look for patterns" rule; a **dated watchlist** for single-channel themes whose *expiries are logged*, so a signal that never corroborated is itself a retained result rather than a hunch rediscovered every quarter; and the observation that **a source that returned nothing because nothing happened and a source that returned nothing because its connector broke are indistinguishable in the corpus**, so each empty source must be annotated with which you believe it was. Its numeric thresholds (3 independent sources, 2 with a tier-A contributor, 90-day watchlist retention, 300-second call floor, ARR and ICP-score bands, cost ceilings) are its own operating parameters and were **deliberately not carried** — our version states the discipline and refuses the numbers. Its tier-weighting idea survives only as the qualified *note who carried a theme, not just how many* line. |
| 2026-08-31 | [cookiy-ai/user-research-skill](https://github.com/cookiy-ai/user-research-skill) (1,531★, MIT, pushed 2026-08-19) — `references/qualitative-research-planner/qualitative-research-planner.md`, `references/synthesize-research-report/phases/*` | MIT | Qualitative research method | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit) | The largest-audience source in this discipline. Four ideas carried: **decision mapping** (name the decision the study will change, or cut the study), the **finite-verb objective rule** (describe / identify / compare / characterize / evaluate — *understand* and *explore* have no finish line, which is why studies built on them are reported as whatever was found), **recruit on behaviour rather than demographics**, and **thematic saturation as the stopping rule** in place of an interview quota. Its sample-size figures (*"5 participants uncover ~80% of usability issues"*, *"8–12 reach saturation"*), session-duration defaults and screener caps were **not** adopted — they are the fixed quotas our new metric explicitly replaces, and the ~80% claim is a contested secondary attribution. Its Cookiy-product references (`cookiy.sh`, billing, recruit, synthetic-user) are vendor-bound; not taken. Its `cookiy-study-synthetic-user.md` is noted as the counter-example our "what never enters a report" list bans. |
| 2026-08-31 | [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) (46,207★, MIT, pushed 2026-08-28) — `skills/customer-research/references/interviews-and-surveys.md`, `source-guides.md` | MIT | Customer research (practitioner) | **enhanced-ours** (co-credit, as the pattern deliberately *bounded*) | Re-read on-thesis. Genuinely good practitioner material — laddering, Sales Safari, disconfirmation over validation — most of which our agent already holds in Rules 1/2/4. The transferable contribution is the one this section **bounds rather than adopts**: its recruiting rule (*interview the customers you want more of* — high deal size, short cycle, low churn) is correct for **language mining** and invalid for **prevalence or churn diagnosis**, because it selects the survivors on purpose. Naming that split — the design licenses the claim — is now the core of our "two silences" passage. Its incentive amounts ($50/call, $5/survey) and its *"aim for 10 calls, be happy with 5"* stopping heuristic were **not** carried: the first is a market figure we cannot verify, the second is the count-instead-of-saturation habit we are replacing. |
| 2026-08-31 | ADD candidates grep-tested and killed: **CRM / marketing-database health** ([Jipup/crm-health-check](https://github.com/Jipup/crm-health-check), MIT; [Growth-Today/claude-skills](https://github.com/Growth-Today/claude-skills) `gt-hubspot-admin` data-hygiene/governance/data-model, MIT; [ariyantoa291/revops-skills](https://github.com/ariyantoa291/revops-skills) `pipeline-hygiene-audit`, MIT) · **marketing forecasting / pipeline coverage** · **cold-email sending infrastructure** ([Growth-Today](https://github.com/Growth-Today/claude-skills) `gt-email-infra`: provisioning, warmup, blacklist-bounce audit, MIT) · **privacy / consent & server-side tracking** | MIT | Ops / analytics / email | **dismissed** (have — recorded so the next ops run does not re-derive) | Database health resolves to `analytics-marketing-ops-architect` (field governance, an authoritative system per fact, the instrumentation contract) plus `email-deliverability-specialist` (list health, bounces, suppression) — a section at most, not a role. Cold-email infrastructure greps to `sales-outbound-strategist` (sending domain, warmup, blacklist) and `email-deliverability-specialist` (SPF/DKIM/DMARC, seed lists, inbox placement) — filed instead as a possible enhancement lead, not a persona. ## 2026-09-01 — paid media / social run (**ADD**: `pmm-brand-demand-strategist` — the out-of-market majority finally has an owner, and "brand awareness" finally has an instrument)

Focus discipline **paid media / social** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-27, the oldest of the four (PMM/sales/GTM 08-28 and 08-29, content/SEO 08-30, email/analytics/ops 08-31). The 08-30 and 08-31 scout runs **both enhanced**, so the alternation rule opened this run looking for a missing role. Thesis set by a grep rather than by a trending repo: **`brand lift`, `brand tracking`, `brand health`, `share of search`, `category entry point`, `dark social`, `demand creation`, `demand capture` and `mental availability` all return zero repo-wide**, `unaided awareness` / `aided awareness` return only the *visual-identity* agent, and **`brand awareness` appears as a stated objective in five agents** (`paid-media-programmatic-buyer`, `paid-media-budget-optimizer`, `paid-media-attribution-analyst`, `design-ad-creative-producer`, `events-field-marketing-strategist`) — the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* pattern, in the place where the most money is spent against the least measurement.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-01 | [etrebels/claude-code-growth-os](https://github.com/etrebels/claude-code-growth-os) (20★, pushed 2026-08-30) — `docs/why-brand.md` | MIT (verified via the GitHub licence API this run) | Brand / demand creation | **adopted-new** (primary) | First sighting and the clearest open-source treatment of demand creation surveyed here. Read in full. Two things carried as ideas: **marketing has two jobs, not one** — harvest the in-market minority, build memory in the out-of-market majority — and the observation that *a team measured only on this quarter's pipeline will starve the second to feed the first*, which is the mechanism behind the budget failure rather than a moral about short-termism. It is also the only source surveyed that flags its own magnitudes as **directional, not benchmark-grade**, in its own text — the discipline this repo has been applying to its Success Metrics for six runs, found in the wild. Its checklist items (name your entry points, pick one distinctive asset and keep it, split the work even 90:10) are ideas we hold differently: our Rule 1 refuses the inherited ratio outright and makes you compute your own. |
| 2026-09-01 | [clawic/skills](https://github.com/clawic/skills) (17★, pushed 2026-07-27) — `skills/cmo/diagnose.md`, sections "Brand Spend Is Under Attack" and "Pipeline Is Down" | MIT (verified this run) | CMO diagnostics | **adopted-new** (co-credit) | First sighting. One idea carried and rebuilt: the three-move answer to *"we can't attribute it"* — name the metric brand is accountable for, name the window, offer a falsifiable test instead of the argument, and **never defend brand spend with impressions**. It appears there as a diagnostic paragraph inside a CMO skill; our Rule 3 makes it a standing rule and adds the requirement that makes it actually work — all three moves are agreed **before** the spend, because a defence assembled after the question is a rationalization and reads as one. Its "brand search volume and share of search are the leading indicators" line corroborated the instrument choice. Not adopted: its pipeline-diagnosis chain and deliverability thresholds, which are `analytics-performance-analyst` and `email-deliverability-specialist` ground. |
| 2026-09-01 | [John Dawes / Ehrenberg-Bass Institute](https://marketingscience.info/news-and-insights/advertising-effectiveness-and-the-95-5-rule-most-b2b-buyers-are-not-in-the-market-right-now) — "Advertising effectiveness and the 95-5 rule" (B2B Institute report, 2021) | Primary source (research institute publication) | Brand / B2B advertising | **adopted** (primary citation) | Read in full this run. Quoted for the **derivation, not the number**: a roughly five-yearly change of provider gives "only 20% of business buyers … 'in the market' over the course of an entire year; something like 5% in a quarter." That it is arithmetic from a purchase cycle is exactly why the new agent's Rule 1 **forbids inheriting 95:5** and requires the reader to redo it with their own contract term and replacement cycle. Also the source for the mechanism claim in Rule 2 — advertising works principally by building a memory link activated when the buyer enters the market. |
| 2026-09-01 | [IPA — "Binet presents fast, cheap, predictive Share of Search metric to EffWorks Global 2020"](https://ipa.co.uk/news/binet-presents-fast-cheap-predictive-share-of-search-metric) | Primary source (trade body report of the presentation) | Brand measurement | **adopted** (primary citation) — *and the source of our own added caveat* | Read in full this run. Supplies the method (share of organic queries within a category brand set, from public trends data), the lead-time observation, and — importantly — the caveats **in the source's own words**: "not a perfect predictor," conversion "affected by other factors, particularly price," and "not all search activity is positive." The detail nobody downstream repeats is that the **validation categories were automotive, energy and mobile handsets** — consumer categories with continuous query volume. Our Rule 6 adds what that implies for B2B: a public trends tool may render nothing at all for a B2B SaaS brand, and **an empty series is no data, not no brand**. Plus the fixed-competitor-set rule and the series-break filing. |
| 2026-09-01 | [rowanbrooks100/brand-strategy-skill](https://github.com/rowanbrooks100/brand-strategy-skill) (14★, pushed 2026-01-18) | MIT (verified this run) | Brand strategy | **dismissed as the mine; one idea credited** | Read in full. A seven-phase agency brand-strategy walkthrough (brand truth → audience architecture → cultural context → messaging → visual language → channel → measurement). Six of its seven phases land on agents we already hold at greater depth — `pmm-positioning-strategist`, `pmm-messaging-architect`, `design-brand-identity-strategist`, `analytics-customer-insights-researcher`. ## 2026-09-02 — PMM / sales / GTM run (**ENHANCE**: `sales-deal-strategist` gains the mutual action plan — the close plan the buyer co-owns)

Focus discipline **PMM / sales / GTM** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-29, the oldest of the four (content/SEO 08-30, email/analytics/ops 08-31, paid media/social 09-01). The 09-01 scout run was an **ADD**, so the alternation rule opened this run looking for a section to improve. Thesis set by a grep rather than by a trending repo: **`mutual action plan`, `mutual success plan`, `close plan`, `buyer enablement` and `buying job` all return zero repo-wide**, in a repo whose deal agent already holds MEDDPICC, a seven-role stakeholder map, Covered/Corroborated/Current thread grading and the JOLT indecision diagnosis — every artifact in the deal belonging to the seller and none to the buyer.

Read for structure only. Three ideas carried and rebuilt in our words: the **backward plan from the buyer's go-live date** rather than forward from the seller's close date; a **named owner on every milestone** with the buyer/vendor split made explicit; and a **single agreed next step** with owner and date rather than a diffuse activity list. Its template layout, headings and prose were not reused. |

Its numeric operating targets (95% plan-logged on commit deals, >90% in-quarter accuracy, written justification above a 5% commit change) were read and **deliberately not adopted**: one author's operating standard, not an evidenced benchmark, and adopting them would violate our anti-fabrication rule. ## 2026-09-03 — content / SEO run (**ADD**: `content-operations-manager` — seven agents made content and nobody ran the line that got it made)

Focus discipline **content / SEO-AEO-GEO** by rotation — last the focus 2026-08-30, the oldest of the four (email/analytics/ops 08-31, paid/social 09-01, PMM/sales/GTM 09-02). The 09-02 scout run **ENHANCED**, so alternation opened this run looking for a missing role. Thesis set by greps rather than by a trending repo: the content discipline held **seven producing specialists and no owner of the production system between them**. `content operations manager`, `managing editor`, `content debt`, `content taxonomy`, `swipe file`, `content sourcing` and `interview-based content` returned **zero** repo-wide outside `maintenance/`; `content ops`, `content governance`, `editorial standards` and `subject matter expert` returned exactly **one file each**. Meanwhile `content-blog-strategist` Rule 3 already required "headline and outline approval before writing" and Rule 7 set a publishing velocity — with nothing in the repo defining the brief, the approval chain, or how capacity is computed. 14 sources evaluated across 8 rows.

| Date | Source | License | Discipline | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-03 | [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (814★, pushed 2026-08-28) — `skills/content-strategy/SKILL.md` | MIT (verified via the GitHub licence API this run) | Content strategy / editorial governance | **adopted-new** (primary program shape) | Second engagement with this source (its `internationalization` skill was co-credited on the 08-30 hreflang ship). Read in full on-thesis. The sharpest treatment surveyed of *governance and lifecycle as a first-class layer of a content program* rather than an afterthought: named production roles (editorial lead, writers, SMEs, editors, SEO, publishers), a per-stage definition of owner / inputs / outputs / quality bar / duration, and two failure patterns worth keeping — **"strategy without capacity"** (a three-a-week plan with one part-time writer) and **"a strategy that never reaches the workflow does not exist."** Ideas only; every sentence in our agent written from scratch. Its keyword/pillar/format layers were **not** carried — `content-blog-strategist` and `seo-keyword-researcher` already own them at greater depth. |

Its generic cadence grids, tooling hooks (Serena/Context7/Playwright) and status-packet formats were not carried. |
